\mu^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}})\Big)+\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)\bigg),

then

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\bigg(1\vee C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\vee\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\leq X\leq k_{1}\wedge\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\bigg)\bigg]<\alpha.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

The structure of this proof is similar to that of Lemma
4
. Define
\mathcal{A}=\{1\vee\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil\vee\Big\lceil\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\Big\rceil,\dots,k_{1}\wedge\lfloor\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\rfloor\}
. With
g(k)
defined as in (
22
), we have

\displaystyle g(k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\bigg(\exp\Big[\frac{c_{\mu}k}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)\Big]-1\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{k}\log\Bigg(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k^{2}_{2}}k\log\Big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=:f^{(1)}(k).

As in the proof of Lemma
4
, will now show that
f^{(1)}(k)
is decreasing in
k
over the set
\mathcal{A}
. It suffices to consider the function
f(k)=:\frac{1}{k}\log\Big(c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k^{2}_{2}}k\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)\Big)
and apply Lemma
29
. By direct calculation, we have

\frac{\textrm{d}f}{\textrm{d}k}(k)=-\frac{\log\big(c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\big)}{k^{2}}-\frac{\log(k)}{k^{2}}+\frac{1}{k^{2}}-\frac{\log\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k^{2}}+\frac{1}{k^{2}\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)}.

The right hand side is less than zero if

1+\frac{1}{\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)}<\log\Big(c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}k}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)\Big)

For
k\in\mathcal{A}
, it holds

\displaystyle\log\Big(c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}k}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\log\Bigg(\frac{c_{\mu}C_{*}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\log\Big(\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}n_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}n_{2}\log\big(\frac{k_{2}^{2}n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\log\Big(\frac{c_{\mu}C_{*}}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq 2

where the second inequality uses that
\frac{\log\log x}{\log x}<\frac{1}{2}
for
x>1
to conclude

\frac{\log\big(\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}n_{1}}{2en_{2}k_{1}^{2}}\big)-\log\log\big(\frac{k_{2}^{2}n_{1}}{n_{2}k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\geq\frac{1}{2},

and the final inequality holds as long as
C_{*}
is taken sufficiently large such that
C_{*}\geq 2e^{2}/c_{\mu}
. Furthermore, for
k\in\mathcal{A}
we have

\displaystyle 1+\frac{1}{\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{1}{\log\big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\big)}

\displaystyle<2

Therefore,
f(k)
is decreasing over
\mathcal{A}
, and by Lemma
29
,
f^{(1)}(k)
is decreasing over
\mathcal{A}
as well. Using our assumed upper bound on
\mu^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}})\Big)+\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(f^{(1)}(k_{1})+f^{(1)}\big(\frac{k_{2}}{\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big(f^{(1)}(k_{1}),f^{(1)}\big(\frac{k_{2}}{\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k\in\mathcal{A}}f^{(1)}(k)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k\in\mathcal{A}}g(k).

We conclude the proof by invoking Lemma
2
.
∎

Lemma 7
.

Suppose that
k_{1}^{2}\geq(2e)^{-4}n_{1}k_{2}
and that
k_{1}<c^{\prime}n_{1}
for some small enough constant
c^{\prime}>0
.
Then for any
\alpha>0
, there exist constants
c_{\mu}>0
and
C_{*}\geq 1
such that the following claims hold. In what follows, we define
c=1-\exp\Big(-c_{\mu}C_{*}(2e)^{-4}\log\Big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\Big)\Big)
.

1.

Suppose that
c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)\leq C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}
. Then if

\mu^{2}<C_{*}^{-1}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\Big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\Big)+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\Big),

it holds

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\big(X\geq\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil\big)\Big]<\alpha.

2.

Suppose that
C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}<c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)\leq k_{1}
. Then if

\mu^{2}<\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{k_{2}n_{1}\log(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})}{c_{\mu}2ek_{1}^{2}}\Big),

it holds

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\big(X\geq\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil\big)\Big]<\alpha.

3.

Suppose that
k_{1}<c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)
.Then if

\mu^{2}<\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}}\big),

it holds

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\big(X\geq\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil\big)\Big]<\alpha.

Proof.

Define
\mathcal{A}=\{\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil,...,k_{1}\}
and
g
as in (
22
). For
k\in\mathcal{A}
, it holds

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\mu}k}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\mu}C_{*}k_{1}^{2}}{k_{2}n_{1}}\log\Big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq c_{\mu}C_{*}(2e)^{-4}\log\Big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\Big)

\displaystyle>0

where the second inequality follows from the assumption
k_{1}^{2}\geq(2e)^{-4}n_{1}k_{2}
and the final inequality holds for
C_{*}>2e
. This implies that
\exp\Big(\frac{c_{\mu}k}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\Big)\Big)
is bounded away from 1 for
k\in\mathcal{A}
. Therefore, for
c=1-\exp\Big(-c_{\mu}C_{*}(2e)^{-4}\log\Big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\Big)\Big)
, it holds

\displaystyle g(k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\bigg(\exp\Big[\frac{c_{\mu}k}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)\Big]-1\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{k}\log\Bigg(1+c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\exp\Big[\frac{c_{\mu}k}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)\Big]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{k}\log\Bigg(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\exp\Big[\frac{c_{\mu}k}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big)\Big]\Bigg)

\displaystyle=:f(k).

Therefore, it suffices to show that
\mu^{2}
is at most the minimum of
f
over
\mathcal{A}
. We write
f(k)
as

f(k)=\frac{1}{k}\log\Big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\Big)+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{kn_{1}}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\Big).

By direct calculation, we have

\frac{\textrm{d}f}{\textrm{d}k}(k)=-\frac{\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)}{k^{2}}+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}k}

from which we deduce that
f
is minimized at
k^{*}=c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)
over
\mathbb{R}^{+}
, decreasing for
k<k^{*}
, and increasing for
k>k^{*}
. We now proceed by cases.

1.

Suppose that
c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)\leq C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}
. Then
f
is increasing over
\mathcal{A}
, and by our assumption on
\mu^{2}
, it holds

\displaystyle\mu^{2}

\displaystyle<C_{*}^{-1}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\Big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\Big)+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k\in\mathcal{A}}f(k)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k\in\mathcal{A}}g(k).

2.

Suppose that
C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}<c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)\leq k_{1}
. Then by our assumption on
\mu^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu^{2}

\displaystyle<\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{k_{2}n_{1}\log(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})}{c_{\mu}2ek_{1}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k\in\mathcal{A}}f(k)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k\in\mathcal{A}}g(k).

3.

Suppose that
k_{1}<c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)
. Then
f
is decreasing over
\mathcal{A}
and is minimized at
k=k_{1}
. Therefore by our assumption on
\mu^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu^{2}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle=\min_{k\in\mathcal{A}}f(k)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k\in\mathcal{A}}g(k).

By Lemma
2
, the proof is complete.
∎

A.4.1
Simplification of the rate

For any
k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, we recall the following quantities:

\displaystyle\psi(k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)

(23)

\displaystyle\phi(k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)&\text{ if }\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\leq 1\\
\infty&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

(24)

\displaystyle\beta(k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\left\{\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)>1\right\}}.

(25)

To alleviate the notation, we will write

\displaystyle\phi_{12}

\displaystyle=\phi(k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2})

\displaystyle\phi_{21}

\displaystyle=\phi(k_{2},k_{1},n_{2},n_{1})

\displaystyle\psi_{12}

\displaystyle=\psi(k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2})

\displaystyle\psi_{21}

\displaystyle=\psi(k_{2},k_{1},n_{2},n_{1})

\displaystyle\beta_{12}

\displaystyle=\beta(k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2})

\displaystyle\beta_{21}

\displaystyle=\beta(k_{2},k_{1},n_{2},n_{1}).

Below, we show that the lower bound can be rewritten as

R:=R(k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2})=\Big(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}\Big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}.

The following lemma simplifies the rate emerging from Lemma
7
.

Lemma 8
.

Assume that
k_{1}^{2}\geq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
for some constant
\bar{c}>0
and
k_{j}\leq c^{\prime}n_{j},\,\forall j\in\{1,2\}
for some sufficiently small constant
c^{\prime}>0
.
Assume also that
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq e\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
.

1.

Then the following two properties hold

\displaystyle\Big(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}\Big)\land\phi_{12}\asymp\psi_{21}\land\phi_{12}

(26)

\displaystyle\psi_{21}\land\phi_{12}\asymp\Big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\Big)\land\phi_{12}.

(27)

2.

It follows that, for any
\alpha>0
, there exists a constant
c_{\mu}>0
such that, whenever
\mu^{2}\leq c_{\mu}R
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\;\mathbf{1}\Big(X\geq C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\Big)\Big]<\alpha.

3.

Moreover, it holds that
R\asymp\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
.

Proof of Lemma
8
.

1.

For any
k_{1},k_{2},n_{1},n_{2}
, we let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{12}=\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right).

We start by showing that

\displaystyle\psi_{21}\land\phi_{12}\asymp\psi_{21}\land\widetilde{\phi}_{12}.

This is clear if
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\leq 1
by definition of
\phi_{12}
.
Assume now that
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}>1
, which implies that
\phi_{12}=\infty
and
\psi_{21}\land\phi_{12}=\psi_{21}
.
By the assumption
k_{1}^{2}\geq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
, we obtain
k_{2}\leq\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}\bar{c}}\leq\frac{1}{\bar{c}}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{12}=\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\bar{c}^{2}n_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{c}^{2}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+n_{2}\right)

by Lemma
31
.(i)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{c}^{2}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}k_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

by Lemma
31
.(ii)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{c}^{2}\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}k_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\bar{c}^{2}\psi_{21}.

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\psi_{21}\land\phi_{12}

\displaystyle\asymp\psi_{21}\land\widetilde{\phi}_{12}

\displaystyle\Big(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}\Big)\land\phi_{12}

\displaystyle\asymp\Big(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}\Big)\land\widetilde{\phi}_{12}

\displaystyle\Big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\Big)\land\phi_{12}

\displaystyle\asymp\Big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\Big)\land\widetilde{\phi}_{12}

and the equations (
26
) and (
27
) become equivalent to proving

\displaystyle\Big(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}\Big)\land\widetilde{\phi}_{12}\asymp\psi_{21}\land\widetilde{\phi}_{12}.

(28)

\displaystyle\psi_{21}\land\widetilde{\phi}_{12}\asymp\Big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\Big)\land\widetilde{\phi}_{12}

(29)

In the rest of the proof, we focus on proving (
28
) and (
29
).

Assume first that
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\leq 1
.
Using the inequality
\log(1+x)\geq x\log(2)
for any
x\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{21}=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\log(2)\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\log(2)}{2}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)\quad\text{ if $c^{\prime}$ is small enough}

\displaystyle=\frac{\log(2)}{2}\widetilde{\phi}_{12},

which guarantees that both (
28
) and (
29
) hold.
From now on, we will therefore assume that
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)>1
.
Now, assume that
\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\leq 1
.
This implies the following inequalities

\displaystyle\psi_{21}=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle>\frac{\log(2)}{k_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\log(2)}{k_{2}}\,\frac{\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\,\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\bar{c}\log(2)\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\bar{c}\log(2)\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bar{c}\log(2)\widetilde{\phi}_{12},

which ensures that (
28
) and (
29
) hold as well and yields the result.
Thus, we will now assume that
\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}>1
, which also implies
\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)>1
.

Now, suppose for the sake of contradiction that
k_{1}>\frac{n_{1}}{\log(n_{1})}
.
Note that the assumption
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq e\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
implies that

\displaystyle\log(n_{1})>e\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)>e\log(\sqrt{n_{2}})=\frac{e}{2}\log(n_{2}),

that is
n_{1}>n_{2}^{e/2}.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle<\frac{k_{2}n_{1}}{n_{1}^{2}/\log^{2}(n_{1})}\log\left(n_{2}\right)<\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}}}{n_{1}}\cdot\log(n_{2})\log^{2}(n_{1})

\displaystyle<\frac{2}{e}\frac{\log^{3}(n_{1})}{n_{1}^{1-1/e}}<1,

which contradicts the assumed inequality
\frac{k_{2}n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)>1
.

Therefore, it holds that
k_{1}\leq\frac{n_{1}}{\log(n_{1})}
.
Since
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)>1
,
we have

\displaystyle\psi_{12}=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(2\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\log(n_{2}/k_{2})+2\log(n_{1})}{k_{1}}\quad\text{ provided }k_{1}\leq c^{\prime}n_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\bar{c}k_{2}}\frac{k_{1}}{n_{1}}\left[\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)+2\log(n_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\bar{c}k_{2}}\left(1/e+2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-1}+2}{\bar{c}\log(2)}\cdot\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right),

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-1}+2}{\bar{c}\log(2)}\psi_{21},

which ensures that (
28
) holds.
Moreover, we verify that equation (
29
) holds as well. This is clear if
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\leq 1
since we have
\beta_{12}=0
. Otherwise, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{21}=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)>\log(2)\frac{1}{k_{2}}\geq\frac{\log(2)\bar{c}}{k_{1}}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq\frac{e\log(2)\bar{c}}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\geq e\bar{c}\log(2)\beta_{12},

which ensures (
29
) holds and concludes the proof of the first claim.

2.

Now, let
\alpha>0
and let
c_{\mu},c>0
be the constants defined in Lemma
7
.

Assume first that
c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)\leq C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}
.
Recalling that
\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{c^{\prime}}
where
c^{\prime}
can be taken arbitrarily small, we have

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{12}=\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(2\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)

provided
c^{\prime}
is small enough

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)

provided
c^{\prime}
is small enough

\displaystyle\leq C_{*}^{-1}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\Big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\Big)+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\Big),

which implies that if
\mu^{2}\leq R
, then we have by Lemma
7
.1. that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\big(X\geq C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\big)\Big]<\alpha.

Now, assume
C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}<c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)\leq k_{1}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\leq\psi_{21}=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(2\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\text{ since }\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\geq 1

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{k_{2}n_{1}\log(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})}{c_{\mu}2ek_{1}^{2}}\Big)

if
c^{\prime}
is small enough.

Therefore, whenever
\mu\leq c_{\mu}R
, we have by Lemma
7
.2. that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\big(X\geq\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil\big)\Big]<\alpha.

Finally, assume that
k_{1}<c_{\mu}^{-1}k_{2}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c_{\mu}}\log\left(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)>\frac{k_{1}}{k_{2}}\geq\frac{1}{16e^{4}}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq\frac{1}{16e^{3}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right),

so that
\frac{k_{2}}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\leq 16e^{3}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\leq\psi_{21}=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}k_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+16e^{3}\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}}\right)\qquad\text{ if $c^{\prime}$ is small enough}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c_{\mu}}\left[\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}}\big)\right]

By Lemma
7
.3, if
\mu^{2}\leq c_{\mu}R\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\big(c\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)+\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}}\big)
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\big(X\geq\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil\big)\Big]<\alpha,

and the proof is complete.

3.

We immediately deduce from (
26
) that
R\gtrsim\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
.
We now prove the converse bound.
Note that if
\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\leq 1
, then we have
\beta_{21}=0
and
\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}=\psi_{12}\lesssim\phi_{21}
by Lemma
13
and the result follows.
We assume that
\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)>1
from now on.

To prove that
R\asymp\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
, it suffices to show that
\beta_{21}\gtrsim\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}
.
We have

\displaystyle\psi_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\frac{k_{2}}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\frac{k_{1}}{\bar{c}n_{1}}\frac{n_{1}}{ek_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\beta_{21}

which concludes the proof.

∎

The Lemma below shows that
R
is a lower bound on the rate emerging from Lemma
4
.

Lemma 9
.

Suppose that
k_{1}^{2}\leq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
for some
\bar{c}\in(0,(2e)^{-4})
and
k_{1}\leq c^{\prime}n_{1}
,
k_{2}\leq c^{\prime}n_{2}
for some sufficiently small
c^{\prime}>0
. Furthermore, suppose that
\frac{k^{2}_{2}}{n_{2}}\leq 2e\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}
.
For some small enough constant
c_{\mu}
, we define the quantity

\displaystyle M=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}})\Big)&\text{ if }k_{1}\leq\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\\
\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

1.

Then for some sufficiently small constant
c>0
depending only on
\mu
, it holds that
M\geq cR
.
Hence, for any
\alpha>0
, there exists a constant
c^{\prime}_{\mu}>0
such that, whenever
\mu^{2}\leq c^{\prime}_{\mu}R
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\Big(1\vee C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\leq X\leq k_{1}\wedge\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\Big)\Big]<\alpha.

2.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle R\geq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21})\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}&\text{ if }k_{1}\leq\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12})\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

(30)

3.

Moreover, it holds that
R\asymp\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
.

Proof of Lemma
9
.

1.

We start by showing that
M\geq c(\psi_{21}+\psi_{12})
.
Using Lemma
4
and Lemma
31
.(i), we obtain

\displaystyle 2M

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}})\Big)+\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\mu}}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}})\Big)+c_{\mu}\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq c_{\mu}\left[\frac{1}{2}\psi_{12}+\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)\right].

To obtain
M\geq c(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21})
, it now remains to show

c\psi_{21}\leq\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big).

Since
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\geq\bar{c}^{-1}\geq 2
, it follows that
\frac{1}{2}\log(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})\leq\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})
by the inequality
\sqrt{1+x}\leq x
that holds true for any
x\geq 2
. Furthermore, the inequality
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\geq\bar{c}^{-1}\geq 16e^{4}
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\log\Big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\Big)}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\log\Big(2e\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)\Big)}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}.

Moreover, since
n_{2}/k_{2}\geq 1/c^{\prime}\geq 4
provided
c^{\prime}\leq 1/4
, we also have that
\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{2k_{2}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
. Combining these observations yields

\displaystyle\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\frac{1}{2}\log\big(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\frac{\log\big(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\Big(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\Big)\right)\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_logk_constants}.(ii)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\psi_{21}.

(31)

It remains to invoke Lemma
4
to conclude the proof of the first claim.

2.

We now prove equation (
30
).
Assume first that
k_{1}\leq\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}
.
Then we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\leq\frac{\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)},\quad\text{ hence }\quad\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\geq\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right),\quad\text{ i.e. }\quad\frac{1}{k_{1}}\geq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)

(32)

by Lemma
30
.(ii).
Note that, since
\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\geq\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq\frac{1}{c^{\prime}}
can be made arbitrarily large by taking
c^{\prime}
small enough, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{21}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)=\psi_{12}.

Hence,

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle=(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21})\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}

\displaystyle\geq\psi_{12}\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21})\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}

as claimed.
Assume now that
k_{1}>\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}
.
By assumption, we have that
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\geq\bar{c}^{-1}\geq 2e
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}}{\log\left(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\right)}\leq\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}},\quad\text{ hence }\quad\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\leq 2\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{2n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right),\quad\text{ i.e. }\quad k_{2}\leq 3k_{1}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)

by Lemma
30
.(i) and for
c^{\prime}
sufficiently small.
This yields

\displaystyle\psi_{12}=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\geq\beta_{12}.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle=(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21})\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12})\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}.

This concludes the proof of equation (
30
).

3.

Assume that
k_{1}\leq\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}
, which implies
k_{2}\geq k_{1}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)
by (
32
). We aim to prove that
\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\gtrsim\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}
. We have

\displaystyle\psi_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\beta_{12}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\psi_{21}=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

∎

The lemma below shows that
R
is a lower bound on the rate emerging from Lemma
5
.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose that
k_{1}^{2}\leq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
for some
\bar{c}\in(0,(2e)^{-4})
and
k_{1}<\frac{1}{2}n_{1}
.
Furthermore, suppose that
\frac{k^{2}_{2}}{n_{2}}\geq 2e\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}
and that
\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil\leq\Big\lfloor\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\Big\rfloor\wedge k_{1}
. Then for any
\alpha>0
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that if
\mu^{2}\leq cR
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\big(\mu^{2}XY\big)\mathbf{1}\bigg(C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\leq X\leq\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\wedge k_{1}\bigg)\right]<\alpha.

Proof of Lemma
10
.

First, observe that the assumption
\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\rceil\leq\big\lfloor\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\big\rfloor\wedge k_{1}
implies

1\leq\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}
. Rearranging terms, this implies

\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\leq C_{*}\log^{-1}\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{n_{2}k_{1}^{2}}\big)\leq\frac{C_{*}}{\log(2e)},

and therefore
\phi_{21}=\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\big(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)
as long as we take the
C
in the definition of
R
to be at least
\frac{C_{*}}{\log(2e)}
. Now, assume that
\mu^{2}\leq\widetilde{c}_{\mu}R
for some
\widetilde{c}_{\mu}>0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}_{\mu}}\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\leq R

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\big(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\big(4e\big)\quad\text{(this follows from $\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\leq 2e\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}$)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\big(4e\big)\log\big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\vee\frac{n_{1}}{2ek^{2}_{1}}\big).

Letting
c^{\prime}_{\mu}
denote the constant from Lemma
5
, we can now choose
\widetilde{c}_{\mu}=\frac{c_{\mu}^{\prime}}{\log(4e)}
sufficiently small so that, whenever
\mu^{2}\leq\widetilde{c}_{\mu}R
, we have
\mu^{2}\leq c_{\mu}^{\prime}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\big(\frac{C_{*}}{2e}\vee\frac{n_{1}}{2ek^{2}_{1}}\big)
.
The conclusion follows by Lemma
5
, and the proof is complete. ∎

The lemma below ensure that
R
is a lower bound on the rate obtained in Lemma
6
.

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that
k_{1}^{2}\leq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
for some
\bar{c}\in(0,(2e)^{-4})
and
k_{1}<c^{\prime}n_{1}
,
k_{2}\leq c^{\prime}n_{2}
for some sufficiently small constant
c^{\prime}
.
Furthermore, suppose that
\frac{k^{2}_{2}}{n_{2}}\geq 2e\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}
.

1.

Then for any
\alpha>0
, there exist constants
c_{\mu}>0
and
C_{*}\geq 1
such that if
1\vee\big\lceil C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\big\rceil\vee\Big\lceil\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\Big\rceil\leq k_{1}\wedge\big\lfloor\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\big\rfloor
and
\mu^{2}\leq c_{\mu}R
,

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\Big(C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\vee\frac{C_{*}k_{2}^{2}/n_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}n_{2}}\big)}\leq X\leq k_{1}\wedge\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\Big)\Big]<\alpha.

2.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle R\geq\begin{cases}\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}&\text{ if }k_{2}\leq k_{1}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\\
\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

Consequently, we have
R\geq\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
.

Proof of Lemma
11
.

1.

By the assumption
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\geq\bar{c}^{-1}\geq 16e^{4}
,
we can repeat the steps leading to equation (
31
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\mu}}{4}\frac{\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\big(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\big)\Big)}{k_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\mu}}{4}\psi_{21}.

It immediately follows that, for some small enough constant
c
, we have

\displaystyle\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\leq cR

\displaystyle\leq c(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log(\frac{n_{1}}{2ek_{1}})\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}{k_{2}}\log\Big(1+c_{\mu}\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{2ek_{1}^{2}\log(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}})}\big)\Big).

The conclusion follows from Lemma
6
.

2.

Assume now that
k_{2}\leq k_{1}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\psi_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\beta_{12},

which yields the desired result.
Assume now that
k_{2}>k_{1}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)
.
Then

\displaystyle\psi_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\beta_{21}.

This concludes the proof.

∎

The lemma below ensure that
R
is a lower bound on the rate obtained in Lemma
3
.

Lemma 12
.

Suppose that
k_{1}^{2}\leq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
for some
\bar{c}\in(0,(2e)^{-4})
and
k_{1}\leq c^{\prime}n_{1}
and
k_{2}\leq c^{\prime}n_{2}
for some small enough constant
c^{\prime}>0
. Furthermore, suppose that
k_{2}<k_{1}\log\big((n_{1}k_{2})/k_{1}^{2}\big)
and
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq e\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
.

1.

Then for any
\alpha_{4}>0
, there exist constants
c_{\mu}>0
and
C_{*}\geq 1
such that if
\mu^{2}\leq c_{\mu}R,
then

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\Big(X\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\lor C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\Big\rceil\Big)\Big]<\alpha_{4}.

2.

Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle R\geq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{4}\big((\psi_{21}+\beta_{12})+(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21})\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}&\text{ if }k_{1}\leq k_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\\
\frac{1}{2}\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Consequently, we have
R\geq\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
.

Proof of Lemma
12
.

1.

Note that the relation
k_{2}<k_{1}\log\big((n_{1}k_{2})/k_{1}^{2}\big)
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle<\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{2n_{1}}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{2n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\quad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_logs}.(i)}

which yields

\displaystyle k_{2}\leq 4k_{1}\log(n_{1}/k_{1}).

(33)

Now, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)=\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\cdot\frac{k_{1}}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{4c^{\prime}}\geq 1

provided
c^{\prime}\leq 1/4
, and

\displaystyle\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{\bar{c}}\log\left(\frac{1}{c^{\prime}}\right)\geq 1

provided
c^{\prime}
and
\bar{c}
are small enough, which ensures that
\nu_{12}=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
and
\beta_{21}=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)
.

Assume first that
k_{1}<k_{2}\log(n_{2}/k_{2})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\leq\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(2\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\frac{k_{1}}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}4\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{2k_{1}}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{8}{e}\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)^{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{2k_{1}}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)+\frac{4}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)+\frac{6}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 6\beta_{12}+6\beta_{21}.

In the second to last inequality, we used the fact that
\log\left(\frac{2k_{1}}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{2k_{1}}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)
due to the inequality
\log(x)\leq x
that holds true for any
x>0
.
It follows from Lemma
3
that if
\mu^{2}\leq c_{\mu}R
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{\mu}>0
, then

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\Big(X\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\lor C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\Big)\Big]<\alpha.

Assume now that
k_{1}>k_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
.
Again, we have
R\leq\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}
.
We aim to show that

\displaystyle\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}\leq\frac{4}{k_{2}}\log\big(\frac{k_{2}n_{1}\log(n_{2}/k_{2})}{2ek_{1}^{2}}\big),

(34)

which will conclude the proof since it ensures that, if
\mu^{2}\leq c_{\mu}R
for some small enough
c_{\mu}>0
, then

\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp(\mu^{2}XY)\mathbf{1}\Big(X\geq\frac{k_{2}}{\log\big(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\big)}\lor C_{*}\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}\Big)\Big]<\alpha.

by Lemma
3
.
We observe that the assumption
k_{1}^{2}\leq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
ensures that
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log(n_{2}/k_{2})>2
, which yields

\displaystyle\psi_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right),

and further implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)\leq\psi_{21}\leq\frac{2}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right).

(35)

Now, by the relations
k_{2}\leq 4k_{1}\log(n_{1}/k_{1})
and
k_{j}\leq c^{\prime}n_{j}
for some small enough constant
c^{\prime}>0
, we also have
\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)>2
, which yields

\displaystyle\psi_{12}=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{2}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right),

so that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)\leq\psi_{12}\leq\frac{2}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)

(36)

To obtain the desired inequality (
34
), it therefore suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

(37)

under the assumptions
\frac{k_{1}}{\bar{c}n_{1}}\leq\frac{k_{2}}{k_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{\log(n_{2}/k_{2})}
and
\frac{n_{j}}{k_{j}}\geq\frac{1}{c^{\prime}}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
, as well as
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq e\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
.

To prove this, we let
x=\frac{k_{2}}{k_{1}}
, and
a_{j}=\frac{n_{j}}{k_{j}}
for
j=1,2
and introduce the function

\displaystyle f(x)=\log\left(a_{1}x\log(a_{2})\right)-x\log\left(\frac{a_{2}\log(a_{1})}{x}\right).

We note that the desired inequality (
37
) is equivalent to showing that
f(x)\geq 0
for any
x\in\left[\frac{1}{\bar{c}a_{1}},\frac{1}{\log(a_{2})}\right]
where
a_{1}\geq e\log(a_{2})
and
a_{1},a_{2}\geq\frac{1}{c^{\prime}}
.
We have, for any
x\in\left[\frac{1}{\bar{c}a_{1}},\frac{1}{\log(a_{2})}\right]

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x}-\log(a_{2}\log(a_{1}))+\log(x)+1

\displaystyle=\log(x)+\frac{1}{x}-\log\left(\frac{a_{2}\log(a_{1})}{e}\right),

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x}-\frac{1}{x^{2}}\leq 0,

since
x\leq\frac{1}{\log(a_{2})}\leq 1
.
Therefore,
f
is concave, and to prove that
f\geq 0
over
\left[\frac{1}{\bar{c}a_{1}},\frac{1}{\log(a_{2})}\right]
, it suffice to show that
f\left(\frac{1}{\bar{c}a_{1}}\right)\geq 0
and
f\left(\frac{1}{\log(a_{2})}\right)\geq 0
.
We have

\displaystyle f\left(\frac{1}{\bar{c}a_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\log(a_{2})}{\bar{c}}\right)-\frac{\log(\bar{c}a_{1})}{\bar{c}a_{1}}-\frac{\log(a_{2})}{\bar{c}a_{1}}-\frac{\log\log(a_{1})}{\bar{c}a_{1}}

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(\frac{\log(a_{2})}{\bar{c}}\right)-\frac{\log(a_{2})}{\bar{c}a_{1}}-1

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\log(a_{2})}{\bar{c}}\right)-1\geq 0.

In the last step, we used the fact that, since
a_{1}\geq e\log(a_{2})
, we also have
\bar{c}a_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log(a_{2})}{\log\log(a_{2}/\bar{c})}

provided
a_{2}
is large enough, which can be enforced by choosing
c^{\prime}>0
small enough.
Similarly, we have

\displaystyle f\left(\frac{1}{\log(a_{2})}\right)

\displaystyle=\log(a_{1})-\frac{1}{\log(a_{2})}\log\Big(a_{2}\log(a_{2})\log(a_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle=\log(a_{1})-1-\frac{\log\log(a_{2})}{\log(a_{2})}-\frac{\log(a_{1})}{\log(a_{2})}

\displaystyle\geq\log(a_{1})-2-\frac{\log(a_{1})}{\log(a_{2})}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

provided
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
are larger than suitably large constants, which can be enforced by choosing
c^{\prime}
small enough.
This concludes the proof of the first claim.

2.

Assume first that
k_{1}<k_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{4k_{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{4k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\big(\beta_{12}+\beta_{21}\big),

which yields
R\geq\frac{1}{8}\big((\psi_{21}+\beta_{12})+(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21})\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
, as desired.

Assume now that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
.
Combining (
35
), (
36
) and (
37
) yields
\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}\asymp\psi_{21}
.
We now show that
\psi_{21}\asymp\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}
.
By assumption, we have

\displaystyle\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{\bar{c}}>1,

which implies that
\beta_{12}=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\beta_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{4k_{1}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)}{k_{2}}\right)

by equation (
33
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{k_{1}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}k_{1}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\right)\right)

using
\log(4x)\leq 2\log(x),~\forall x\geq 4

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{k_{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

by equation (
37
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\psi_{21}

by equation (
35
).

Therefore, we have
\psi_{12}+\psi_{21}\geq\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}/2
, hence
R\geq\frac{1}{2}\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
and the proof is complete.

∎

Lemma 13
.

Assume that, for some constant
c>0
, we have
k_{2}\leq cn_{2}
.
If
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\leq 1
, then we have
\psi_{21}\leq\phi_{12}
.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

Note that, when
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\leq 1
, we have
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\leq 1
, so that
\phi_{12}=\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)
. We obtain

\displaystyle\psi_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\phi_{12},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 14
.

It holds that
R\gtrsim\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}
.

Proof of Lemma
14
.

Assume first that, for some constant
c>0
, we have
k_{1}\geq cn_{1}
or
k_{2}\geq cn_{2}
, and by symmetry, assume we have
k_{1}\geq cn_{1}
.
Then

\displaystyle\psi_{12}=\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)\geq\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)\asymp\phi_{12},

which yields that

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle=(\psi_{12}+\psi_{21})\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}

\displaystyle\asymp\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}

\displaystyle\geq\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}.

From now on, assume that
k_{1}\leq cn_{1}
and
k_{2}\leq cn_{2}
, and assume without loss of generality that
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq e\log\left(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}\right)
, by lemma
27
.

If
k_{1}^{2}\geq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
, then the result follows by Lemma
8
. Now, assume that
k_{1}^{2}<\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
. If
\frac{k^{2}_{2}}{n_{2}}\leq 2e\frac{k_{1}^{2}}{n_{1}}
, then the result follows by Lemma
9
.
Else, we have
k_{1}^{2}\geq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
and the result follows by Lemmas
11
and
12
.

∎

Appendix B
Proofs for upper bound

B.1
Analysis of total degree test

Recall that the total degree test is defined as
\Delta_{\text{deg}}^{h}=\mathbf{1}(t_{\text{deg}}>h)
for a choice of threshold
h>0
, where

t_{\text{deg}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{n_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}(A_{ij}-p_{0})

(38)

Lemma 15
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
be given and define
h_{\alpha}=\sqrt{4\log(2/\alpha)}
. Suppose that
n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}\geq\frac{4}{27}h_{\alpha}^{2}
and
p_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}]
. Then there exists a constant
C_{\delta}>0
such that if

\delta^{2}\geq C_{\delta}p_{0}(1-p_{0})\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}},

then the linear test with threshold
h=h_{\alpha}
satisfies

\mathcal{R}(\Delta_{\text{deg}}^{h},\delta)\leq\alpha.

Proof of Lemma
15
.

We begin by an analysis of the Type 1 error of
\Delta_{\text{deg}}^{h}
. Under the null hypothesis, it holds that
\mathbb{E}[t_{\text{deg}}]=0
and
\textrm{var}(t_{\text{deg}})=1
. Let
\sigma^{2}=p_{0}(1-p_{0})n_{1}n_{2}
. Note that under our assumptions
n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}\geq\frac{4}{27}h_{\alpha}^{2}
and
p_{0}\leq\frac{1}{4}
, it holds

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=\sqrt{p_{0}(1-p_{0})n_{1}n_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}p_{0}n_{1}n_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}\frac{4}{27}h^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}h

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Delta_{\text{deg}}^{h}=1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{0}(t_{\text{deg}}>h)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{0}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}(A_{ij}-p_{0})>\sigma h\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\sigma^{2}+\frac{1}{3}h\sigma}\Big)\quad\text{(by Bernstein's inequality)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}h^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\Big)\quad\text{(since $\sigma\geq\frac{1}{3}h$)}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{4}h^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-\log(2/\alpha)\big)\quad\text{(since $h=\sqrt{4\log(2/\alpha)}$)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{2}.

Now we turn our attention to the Type 2 error. Under the alternative hypothesis, there exist subsets of indices
K_{1}\subset[n_{1}]
and
K_{1}\subset[n_{2}]
of sizes
|K_{1}|=k_{1}
and
|K_{2}|=k_{2}
such that
\mathbb{E}[A_{ij}]=p_{0}+\delta
for
(i,j)\in K_{1}\times K_{2}
and
\mathbb{E}[A_{ij}]=p_{0}
otherwise. Since the
\{A_{ij}\}_{i\in[n_{1}],j\in[n_{2}]}
are independent Bernoulli random variables, we can directly compute

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}[t_{\text{deg}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma}k_{1}k_{2}\delta,

\displaystyle\textrm{var}_{\mathbf{P}}(t_{\text{deg}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\big(k_{1}k_{2}p_{1}(1-p_{1})+(n_{1}-k_{1})(n_{2}-k_{2})p_{0}(1-p_{0})\big),

where we let
p_{1}=p_{0}+\delta
. Now under the assumption that
\delta^{2}\geq C_{\delta}p_{0}(1-p_{0})\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}}
, it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}[t_{\text{deg}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma}k_{1}k_{2}\delta

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}k_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}}\delta

\displaystyle\geq C_{\delta}.

Therefore, for
C_{\delta}
taken large enough, we can ensure that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}[t_{\text{deg}}]\geq 2h=2\sqrt{4\log(2/\alpha)}
. Therefore, we may perform the following calculation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}(\Delta_{\text{deg}}^{h}=0)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}(t_{\text{deg}}\leq h)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}(t_{\text{deg}}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}}\leq h-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big((t_{\text{deg}}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}})^{2}\geq(h-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big((t_{\text{deg}}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{\textrm{var}_{\mathbf{P}}(t_{\text{deg}})}{(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}})^{2}},

where the final inequality uses Markov’s. From here, we apply our closed form expressions of
\textrm{var}_{\mathbf{P}}(t_{\text{deg}})
and
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}[t_{\text{deg}}]
derived above to compute:

\displaystyle\frac{\textrm{var}_{\mathbf{P}}(t_{\text{deg}})}{(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}k_{2}p_{1}(1-p_{1})+(n_{1}-k_{1})(n_{2}-k_{2})p_{0}(1-p_{0})}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{1}k_{2}(p_{0}+\delta)+\sigma^{2}}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle=\text{I}+\text{II}+\text{III},

where we define
\text{I}=\frac{p_{0}}{k_{1}k_{2}\delta^{2}}
,
\text{II}=\frac{1}{k_{1}k_{2}\delta}
, and
\text{III}=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{k^{2}_{1}k^{2}_{2}\delta^{2}}
. We control each of these terms separately. First, we have

I

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{0}}{k_{1}k_{2}\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{1}k_{2}p_{0}}{n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}(1-p_{0})C_{\delta}}\quad\text{(since $\delta^{2}\geq C_{\delta}\frac{n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}{k^{2}_{1}k^{2}_{2}}$ )}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{3C_{\delta}}\quad\text{(since $k_{i}\leq n_{i}$ for $i\in\{1,2\}$ and $1-p_{0}\geq\frac{3}{4}$)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{24},

where the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large. Now we consider
II
. Recall that
n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}\geq\frac{4}{27}h^{2}=\frac{8}{27}\log(2/\alpha)
. Therefore,
p_{0}\geq\frac{C}{n_{1}n_{2}}
where
C>0
is a constant that depends on
\alpha
. We thus have

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{C_{\delta}p_{0}(1-p_{0})\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{C_{\delta}\frac{3}{4}}\sqrt{p_{0}\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}}}\quad\text{(since $1-p_{0}\geq\frac{3}{4}$)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C^{\prime}}{k_{1}k_{2}},

where
C^{\prime}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}C_{\delta}C}
. Using this lower bound on
\delta
, we have

II

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}k_{2}\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{24}.

where the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large. Finally, to control
III
we have

III

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{k^{2}_{1}k^{2}_{2}\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}(1-p_{0})k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}}{C_{\delta}n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}(1-p_{0})k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}}\quad\text{(since $\delta^{2}\geq C_{\delta}\frac{n_{1}n_{2}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}{k^{2}_{1}k^{2}_{2}}$)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C_{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{24}

where the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken large enough. Combining these bounds on
\text{I},\text{II},
and
III
gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}(\Delta_{\text{deg}}^{h}=0)

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{\textrm{var}_{\mathbf{P}}(t_{\text{deg}})}{(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}t_{\text{deg}})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 4(\text{I}+\text{II}+\text{III})

\displaystyle\leq 4(3\frac{\alpha}{24})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{2}.

Combining this inequality with our bound on the Type I error, we have

\mathcal{R}(\Delta_{\text{deg}}^{h},\delta)\leq\alpha,

and the proof is complete.
∎

B.2
Analysis of the truncated degree test

The following lemma controls the risk of
\Delta^{h}_{\text{trunc-deg},1}
. The analysis of
\Delta^{h}_{\text{trunc-deg},2}
is analogous.

Lemma 16
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
and define
h_{\alpha}=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{n_{2}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\log(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}})\right)\log(2/\alpha)}+\log(2/\alpha)\right)
for constants
c^{\prime},C^{*}>0
to be determined later. Suppose that
n_{2}\geq ck_{2}^{2}
for a constant
c>0
. Then there exist constants
C_{\delta},C_{\alpha}>0
and
C\geq\frac{8}{3}
such that if
\frac{1}{4}\geq p_{0}\geq\frac{C_{\alpha}}{n_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)
and

\delta^{2}\geq C_{\delta}p_{0}(1-p_{0})\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right),

then the truncated degree test with threshold
h=h_{\alpha}
and
\tau=\sqrt{C\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)}
satisfies

\mathcal{R}(\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{trunc-deg},1},\delta)\leq\alpha.

Proof of Lemma
16
.

First, we control the Type 1 error of
\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{trunc-deg},1}
. Let
\sigma=\sqrt{n_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}
. By Lemma
19
, there exist constants
C^{\prime},c,c^{\prime},C^{*}>0
such that if
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{trunc-deg},1}>h_{\alpha})\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}.

We impose that
c^{\prime}\geq 1
by taking
C
in the definition of
\tau
sufficiently large. Therefore we just need to verify that
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
. Since
n_{2}\geq ck_{2}^{2}
, it holds

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\sqrt{C\log(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}})}

\displaystyle>\sqrt{C\log(1+c)}

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime},

where the final inequality holds for
C
taken large enough. On the other hand, since
\frac{1}{4}\geq p_{0}\geq\frac{C_{\alpha}}{n_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle c\sigma

\displaystyle=c\sqrt{n_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}

\displaystyle\geq c\sqrt{C_{\alpha}\frac{3}{4}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{C\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)}\quad\text{(for $C_{\alpha}$ large enough)}

\displaystyle=\tau.

Therefore
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
, and the desired bound on the Type 1 error holds.

Now we aim to control the Type 2 error. Under the alternative hypothesis, there exist sets
K_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{k_{1}}(n_{1})
and
K_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{k_{2}}(n_{2})
such that
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}]=\mathbf{P}
with entries
P_{ij}\geq p_{0}+\delta
if
(i,j)\in K_{1}\times K_{2}
and
P_{ij}=p_{0}
otherwise. Let
\theta=\frac{k_{1}\delta}{\sigma}
. Then by Lemma
20
, there exist constants
\tilde{c},c,C^{\prime}>0
such that if
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
,
k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)\geq C^{\prime}
, and
\theta\geq C^{\prime}\tau
, then

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(\bar{A}_{j}>\tau)\right]\geq\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right).

(39)

As when we controlled the Type 1 error, we enforce
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
by taking
C
in the definition of
\tau
and
C_{\alpha}
sufficiently large, in that order. To verify
k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)\geq C^{\prime}
, we calculate

\displaystyle k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)

\displaystyle\geq k_{1}\delta

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{n_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})C_{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{CC_{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)}\quad\text{(since $\sigma\gtrsim 1$, as shown above)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{CC_{\delta}\log\left(1+c\right)}

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime},

where the finally inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large. Finally, to verify
\theta\geq C^{\prime}\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}\delta}{\sqrt{n_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{C_{\delta}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)}

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime}\sqrt{C\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)}\quad\text{(for $C_{\delta}$ taken large enough)}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}\tau.

Therefore, (
39
) holds by Lemma
20
. Additionally, by Lemma
21
, there exist constants
C_{1},c,C^{\prime}>0
such that if
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
,
k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)\geq C^{\prime}
, and
\theta\geq 2\tau
, then

\textrm{var}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(\bar{A}_{j}>\tau)\right)\leq C_{1}k_{2}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)+C_{1}(n_{2}-k_{2})\left(\sigma\tau\log(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma})\right)^{2}\exp\big(-\frac{3}{8}\tau^{2}\big).

(40)

As before, we enforce
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
,
k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)\geq C^{\prime}
, and
\theta\geq 2\tau
by taking
C
,
C_{\alpha}
, and
C_{\delta}
sufficiently large in that order. To control the Type 2 error with (
39
) and (
40
) and Chebyshev’s inequality, we need to verify that the right-hand side of (
39
) is at least
2h_{\alpha}
. In what follows, we define

\bar{\delta}^{2}:=C_{\delta}p_{0}(1-p_{0})\frac{n_{1}}{k^{2}_{1}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big),

so that
\delta\geq\bar{\delta}
. First, we have

\displaystyle\log\Big(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\Big)

\displaystyle=\log\Big(1+\frac{k_{1}\delta}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\log\Big(1+\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big).

We now aim to show that there exists a constant
\bar{C}>0
such that
\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\bar{C}
. By the definitions of
\bar{\delta}
and
\sigma^{2}
, it suffices to show that

\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)\leq\bar{C}\sigma^{2}.

Recall that
\tau^{2}=C\log\big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\big)
. Since we have already shown that
\tau^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}
, it holds that there exists a constant
\bar{C}>0
such that
\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)\leq\bar{C}\sigma^{2}
as desired. Therefore, there exists a constant
\bar{c}>0
such that
\log\big(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\big)\geq\log\big(1+\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma}\big)\geq\bar{c}\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma}
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq ck_{2}\theta k_{1}\bar{\delta}\quad\text{(for $c=\tilde{c}\bar{c}$)}

\displaystyle\geq ck_{2}\frac{(k_{1}\bar{\delta})^{2}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=cC_{\delta}k_{2}\sigma\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime}_{\delta}k_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big),

where the final inequality uses
\sigma\gtrsim 1
, and
C^{\prime}_{\delta}>0
is a positive multiple of
C_{\delta}
. Since we have enforced
c^{\prime}\geq 1
in the definition of
h_{\alpha}
, it holds

\displaystyle h_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{n_{2}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\log(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}})\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{*}\left(k_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log(2/\alpha)}{1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{*}\left(k_{2}\sqrt{\log(2/\alpha)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{\delta}}{2}\left(k_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)\right),

where the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large, and uses that
\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\geq c>0
. Therefore, we have shown that for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large, it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(\bar{A}_{j}>\tau)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 2h_{\alpha}.

Letting
U_{:}=\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(\bar{A}_{j}>\tau)
, we therefore can conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big(\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{trunc-deg},1}=0\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big(U\leq h_{\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big(U-\mathbb{E}[U]\leq h_{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}[U]\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\Big(\big(U-\mathbb{E}[U]\big)^{2}\geq\big(h_{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}[U]\big)^{2}\Big)\quad\text{(since $\mathbb{E}[U]\geq 2h_{\alpha}$)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\Big(\big(U-\mathbb{E}[U]\big)^{2}\geq\big(\mathbb{E}[U]\big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\textrm{var}\big(U\big)}{\big(\mathbb{E}[U]\big)^{2}}\quad\text{(by Markov's inequality)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}k_{2}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)+C_{1}(n_{2}-k_{2})\left(\sigma\tau\log(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma})\right)^{2}\exp\big(-\frac{3}{8}\tau^{2}\big)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\text{I}+\text{II},

where we define

\text{I}=\frac{C_{1}k_{2}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}},

and

\text{II}=\frac{C_{1}(n_{2}-k_{2})\left(\sigma\tau\log(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma})\right)^{2}\exp\big(-\frac{3}{8}\tau^{2}\big)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}}.

We control these terms separately. First we have

I

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}k_{2}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)}{\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\theta^{2}}+\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\theta\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\sqrt{\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{1}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\sqrt{\log(1+c)}}+\frac{C_{1}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\log(1+c)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{4},

where the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken large enough. Recall that
C\geq\frac{8}{3}
in the definition of
\tau
. Then it holds

II

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}(n_{2}-k_{2})\left(\sigma\tau\log(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma})\right)^{2}\exp\big(-\frac{3}{8}\tau^{2}\big)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}n_{2}}{\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}^{2}\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)}\cdot\frac{\left(\tau\log\big(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma}\big)\right)^{2}}{\left(\theta\log\big(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\big)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\left(C\theta\log(1+c)\right)^{2}}{\left(\theta\log\big(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\big)\right)^{2}}\quad\text{(since $\tau\leq c\sigma$ and $\tau\leq C\theta$)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\left(C\log(1+c)\right)^{2}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\left(k_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\Big)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\left(C\log(1+c)\right)^{2}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\left(k_{2}\log\Big(1+c\Big)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{4},

where again the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large. Therefore, we have shown

\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big(\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{trunc-deg},1}=0\big)\leq\text{I}+\text{II}\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}.

Combining this with our bound on the Type 1 error, it holds

\mathcal{R}(\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{trunc-deg},1},\delta)\leq\alpha,

and the proof is complete.
∎

B.3
Analysis of the max truncated degree test

The following lemma controls the risk of
\Delta^{h}_{\text{max-trunc-deg},1}
. The analysis of
\Delta^{h}_{\text{max-trunc-deg},2}
is analogous.

Lemma 17
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
and define

h_{\alpha}=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{n_{2}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}+\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)

for constants
c^{\prime},C^{*}>0
to be determined later. Suppose that
\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}>c
for a constant
c\geq 0
. Then there exist constants
C_{\delta},C_{\alpha}>0
and
C\geq\frac{8}{3}
such that if
\frac{1}{4}\geq p_{0}\geq\frac{C_{\alpha}}{k_{1}k_{2}}\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)
and

\delta^{2}\geq C_{\delta}p_{0}(1-p_{0})\Bigg(\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\frac{1}{k_{2}k_{1}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Bigg),

then the truncated degree test with threshold
h=h_{\alpha}
and
\tau=\sqrt{C\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}
satisfies

\mathcal{R}(\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{trunc-deg},1},\delta)\leq\alpha.

Proof of Lemma
17
.

Let
\sigma=\sqrt{k_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}
. We begin by controlling the Type 1 error.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{max-trunc-deg},1}=1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{0}(t_{\text{max-trunc-deg},1}>h_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(\max\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{J_{1},j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(t_{J_{1},j}>\tau)\,\Big|\,J_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{k_{1}}(n_{1})\right\}>h_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{J_{1},j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(t_{J_{1},j}>\tau)>h_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\exp\left(-\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{2},

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
19
as long as
h_{\alpha}
is defined with the constants
C^{*}
and
c^{\prime}
obtained from Lemma
19
and
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
for some constants
C^{\prime},c>0
. We impose that the constant
c^{\prime}
is made to be greater than
1
by taking the constant
C
in the definition of
\tau
sufficiently large. Therefore, we just need to verify that
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
to finalize our control of the Type 1 error. First, we have

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\sqrt{C\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle>\sqrt{C\log\left(1+c\right)}

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime},

where the final inequality holds for
C
taken sufficiently large. Additionally, using
\frac{1}{4}\geq p_{0}\geq\frac{C_{\alpha}}{k_{1}k_{2}}\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)
we have

\displaystyle c\sigma

\displaystyle=c\sqrt{k_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}

\displaystyle\geq c\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}C_{\alpha}\log\left(e\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{C\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}\quad\text{(for $C_{\alpha}$ large enough)}

\displaystyle=\tau.

Therefore
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
, and the bound on the Type 1 error holds.

Now we turn our attention to the Type 2 error. Under the alternative hypothesis, there exist sets
K_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{k_{1}}(n_{1})
and
K_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{k_{2}}(n_{2})
such that
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}]=\mathbf{P}
with entries
P_{ij}\geq p_{0}+\delta
if
(i,j)\in K_{1}\times K_{2}
and
P_{ij}=p_{0}
otherwise. Let
\theta=\frac{k_{1}\delta}{\sigma}
. Then by Lemma
20
, there exist constants
\tilde{c},c,C^{\prime}>0
such that if
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
,
k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)\geq C^{\prime}
, and
\theta\geq C^{\prime}\tau
, then

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{K_{1},j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(t_{K_{1},j}>\tau)\right]\geq\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right).

(41)

As when we controlled the Type 1 error, we enforce
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
by taking
C
in the definition of
\tau
and
C_{\alpha}
sufficiently large, in that order. To verify
k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)\geq C^{\prime}
, we calculate

\displaystyle k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)

\displaystyle\geq k_{1}\delta

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{k_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})C_{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}C_{\alpha}\log\left(e\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)C_{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}C_{\alpha}C_{\delta}\log\left(1+c\right)}

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime},

where the finally inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large. Finally, to verify
\theta\geq C^{\prime}\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}\delta}{\sqrt{k_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{C_{\delta}\left(\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime}\sqrt{C\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)}\quad\text{(for $C_{\delta}$ taken large enough)}

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}\tau.

Therefore, (
41
) holds due to Lemma
20
. Additionally, by Lemma
21
, there exist constants
C_{1},c,C^{\prime}>0
such that if
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
,
k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)\geq C^{\prime}
, and
\theta\geq 2\tau
, then

\textrm{var}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{K_{1},j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(t_{K_{1},j}>\tau)\right)\leq C_{1}k_{2}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)+C_{1}(n_{2}-k_{2})\left(\sigma\tau\log(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma})\right)^{2}\exp\big(-\frac{3}{8}\tau^{2}\big).

(42)

As before, we enforce
\tau\in[C^{\prime},c\sigma]
,
k_{1}(p_{0}+\delta)\geq C^{\prime}
, and
\theta\geq 2\tau
by taking
C
,
C_{\alpha}
, and
C_{\delta}
sufficiently large in that order. We aim to control the Type 2 error by combining (
41
) and (
42
) in an application of Chebyshev’s inequality. Before we can do this, we need to verify that the right-hand side of (
41
) is at least
2h_{\alpha}
. In what follows, we define

\bar{\delta}^{2}:=C_{\delta}p_{0}(1-p_{0})\Bigg(\frac{1}{k_{1}}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\frac{1}{k_{2}k_{1}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Bigg),

so that
\delta\geq\bar{\delta}
by assumption. First, we have

\displaystyle\log\Big(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\Big)

\displaystyle=\log\Big(1+\frac{k_{1}\delta}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\log\Big(1+\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big).

We now aim to show that there exists a constant
\bar{C}>0
such that
\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\bar{C}
. By the definitions of
\bar{\delta}
and
\sigma^{2}
, it suffices to show that

\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\leq\bar{C}\sigma^{2}.

Recall that
\tau^{2}=C\log\big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\big)
.
Since we have already shown that
\tau\leq c\sigma
, it is clear that
\log\big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\big)\leq C^{\prime}\sigma^{2}
for some
C^{\prime}>0
. Furthermore, by the assumption
\frac{1}{4}\geq p_{0}\geq\frac{C_{\alpha}}{k_{1}k_{2}}\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)
, it holds

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=k_{1}p_{0}(1-p_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}\frac{C_{\alpha}}{k_{2}}\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}\frac{C_{\alpha}}{k_{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}},

and hence
k_{2}^{-1}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\sigma^{2}
for some
C^{\prime\prime}>0
. Taking
\bar{C}=(C^{\prime}+C^{\prime\prime})/C_{\delta}
, we have that
\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\bar{C}
. Therefore, there exists a constant
\bar{c}>0
such that
\log\big(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\big)\geq\log\big(1+\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma}\big)\geq\bar{c}\frac{k_{1}\bar{\delta}}{\sigma}
. With this result in hand, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq ck_{2}\theta k_{1}\bar{\delta}\quad\text{(for $c=\tilde{c}\bar{c}$)}

\displaystyle\geq ck_{2}\frac{(k_{1}\bar{\delta})^{2}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=cC_{\delta}k_{2}\sigma\left(\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq C^{\prime}_{\delta}\left(k_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right),

where the final inequality uses
\sigma\gtrsim 1
, and
C^{\prime}_{\delta}>0
is a positive multiple of
C_{\delta}
. Note that, since we have enforced
c^{\prime}\geq 1
in the definition of
h_{\alpha}
, it holds

\displaystyle h_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{n_{2}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\log(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}})\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}+\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{*}\left(k_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}}{1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}}}+\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{*}\left(k_{2}+\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{\delta}}{2}\left(k_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right),

where the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large, and uses that
\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\geq c>0
. To summarize, we have shown that for
C_{\delta}
taken large enough, it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{K_{1},j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(t_{K_{1},j}>\tau)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 2h_{\alpha}.

Letting
U_{K_{1}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}\big(W_{K_{1},j}-\nu^{k_{1}}_{\tau}\big)\mathbf{1}(t_{K_{1},j}>\tau)
, we therefore can conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big(t_{\text{max-trunc-deg}}\leq h_{\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big(U_{K_{1}}\leq h_{\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big(U_{K_{1}}-\mathbb{E}[U_{K_{1}}]\leq h_{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}[U_{K_{1}}]\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\Big(\big(U_{K_{1}}-\mathbb{E}[U_{K_{1}}]\big)^{2}\geq\big(h_{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}[U_{K_{1}}]\big)^{2}\Big)\quad\text{(since $\mathbb{E}[U_{K_{1}}]\geq 2h_{\alpha}$)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\Big(\big(U_{K_{1}}-\mathbb{E}[U_{K_{1}}]\big)^{2}\geq\big(\mathbb{E}[U_{K_{1}}]\big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\textrm{var}\big(U_{K_{1}}\big)}{\big(\mathbb{E}[U_{K_{1}}]\big)^{2}}\quad\text{(by Markov's inequality)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}k_{2}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)+C_{1}(n_{2}-k_{2})\left(\sigma\tau\log(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma})\right)^{2}\exp\big(-\frac{3}{8}\tau^{2}\big)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\text{I}+\text{II},

where we define

\text{I}=\frac{C_{1}k_{2}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}},

and

\text{II}=\frac{C_{1}(n_{2}-k_{2})\left(\sigma\tau\log(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma})\right)^{2}\exp\big(-\frac{3}{8}\tau^{2}\big)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}}.

We control each of these terms separately. First we have

I

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}k_{2}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma)}{\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\theta^{2}\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\theta^{2}}+\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\theta\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\sqrt{\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{1}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\left(\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\sqrt{\log(1+c)}}+\frac{C_{1}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}\log(1+c)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{4},

where the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken large enough. Now we control
II
. Recall that
C\geq\frac{8}{3}
in the definition of
\tau
. Then it holds

II

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}(n_{2}-k_{2})\left(\sigma\tau\log(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma})\right)^{2}\exp\big(-\frac{3}{8}\tau^{2}\big)}{\left(\tilde{c}k_{2}\theta\sigma\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}n_{2}}{\tilde{c}^{2}k_{2}^{2}\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)}\cdot\frac{\left(\tau\log\big(1+\frac{\tau}{\sigma}\big)\right)^{2}}{\left(\theta\log\big(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\big)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\left(C\theta\log(1+c)\right)^{2}}{\left(\theta\log\big(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\big)\right)^{2}}\quad\text{(since $\tau\leq c\sigma$ and $\tau\leq C\theta$)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\left(C\log(1+c)\right)^{2}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\left(k_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\Big)+\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\tilde{c}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\left(C\log(1+c)\right)^{2}}{C^{\prime}_{\delta}\left(k_{2}\log\Big(1+c\Big)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{4},

where again the final inequality holds for
C_{\delta}
taken sufficiently large. Therefore, we have shown

\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{P}}\big(t_{\text{max-trunc-deg},1}\leq h_{\alpha}\big)\leq\text{I}+\text{II}\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}.

Combining this with our bound on the Type 1 error, it holds

\mathcal{R}(\Delta^{h_{\alpha}}_{\text{max-trunc-deg},1},\delta)\leq\alpha,

and the proof is complete.
∎

B.4
Proof of Theorem 2

Here, we provide the proof of Theorem 2, which gives an upper bound on the minimax rate of separation
\delta^{*}
.
We let

\displaystyle\tilde{R}=\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\land\phi_{12}\land\phi_{21}.

We define our optimal test as

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}=\begin{cases}\Delta^{h_{3}}_{\text{max-trunc-deg},1}&\text{ if }\tilde{R}=\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\\
\Delta^{h_{3}}_{\text{max-trunc-deg},2}&\text{ if }\tilde{R}=\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\\
\Delta_{a}^{h_{1},h_{2}}&\text{ if }\tilde{R}=\phi_{12}\\
\Delta_{b}^{h_{1}^{\prime},h_{2}^{\prime}}&\text{ if }\tilde{R}=\phi_{21},\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{a}^{h_{1},h_{2}}=\begin{cases}\Delta^{h_{1}}_{\text{trunc-deg},1}&\text{if $\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\geq c_{1}$,}\\
\Delta^{h_{2}}_{\text{deg}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\Delta_{b}^{h_{1}^{\prime},h_{2}^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}\Delta^{h_{1}^{\prime}}_{\text{trunc-deg},2}&\text{if $\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\geq c_{1}$,}\\
\Delta^{h_{2}^{\prime}}_{\text{deg}}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

and, for some small enough
\alpha>0
, we let

\displaystyle h_{3}
