\displaystyle=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{n_{2}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\log{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)}+\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle h_{4}

\displaystyle=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{n_{1}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)\right)\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)}+\log\left(\frac{2}{\alpha}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle h_{1}

\displaystyle=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{n_{2}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\log(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}})\right)\log(2/\alpha)}+\log(2/\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle h_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{n_{1}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\log(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}})\right)\log(2/\alpha)}+\log(2/\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle h_{2}

\displaystyle=h_{2}^{\prime}=\sqrt{4\log(2/\alpha)}.

We recall the assumption

\displaystyle p_{0}\geq\begin{cases}\frac{C_{\eta}}{k_{1}k_{2}}\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)&\text{ if }\tilde{R}=\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)\\[5.0pt]
\frac{C_{\eta}}{n_{1}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)&\text{ if }\tilde{R}=\phi_{12}\text{ and }n_{2}>k_{2}^{2}\\[5.0pt]
\frac{C_{\eta}}{n_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\right)&\text{ if }\tilde{R}=\phi_{21}\text{ and }n_{1}>k_{1}^{2}\\[5.0pt]
\frac{C_{\eta}}{n_{1}n_{2}}&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

By Lemma
14
, we always have
R\gtrsim\tilde{R}
.
Assume first that
\tilde{R}=\big(\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}\big)\land\big(\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}\big)
, and, by symmetry, assume that we have
\tilde{R}=\psi_{12}+\beta_{21}
.
In this case, we have
\Delta^{*}=\Delta^{h_{3}}_{\text{max-trunc-deg},1}
, and the result follows by Lemma
17
. We can proceed similarly in the case where
\tilde{R}=\psi_{21}+\beta_{12}
.

Assume now that
\tilde{R}=\phi_{12}
and
n_{2}>k_{2}^{2}
. Then the result follows by Lemma
16
. Similarly, if

\tilde{R}=\phi_{21}
and
n_{1}>k_{1}^{2}
, the result follows by Lemma
16
.
Finally, if none of the conditions above are satisfied, then we have
\Delta^{*}=\Delta^{h_{2}}_{\text{deg}}
and the result follows by Lemma
15
.
The proof is complete.

Appendix C
Additional results

C.1
Technical results for the analysis of the truncated chi-square tests

Lemma 18
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
p\in(0,1/4)
.
Let
X\sim\operatorname{Bin}(n,p)
and, for any
a>0
, define

\displaystyle Z\,=\,(X-np)/\sigma,\qquad\text{ where }\sigma=\sqrt{np(1-p)}

\displaystyle v(x)=\sigma x\log\left(1+\frac{x}{\sigma}\right),\quad\forall x>0.

For any
\alpha\geq 1
, there exist two constants
C_{\alpha},\bar{C}>0
such that, for any
a\in\big[C_{\alpha},\sigma\big]
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[v(Z)^{\alpha}\big|Z\geq a\right]\leq\bar{C}v(a)^{\alpha}
.

Proof of Lemma
18
.

We first note that the event
\{Z\geq a\}
is equivalent to
\{X\geq k_{\min}\}
where
k_{\min}=\lceil np+a\sigma\rceil
. Let
c^{*}>0
be a constant whose value will be adjusted later. Let also
\alpha>0
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[v(Z)^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}{\big(Z\geq a\big)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[v(Z)^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}{\big(X\geq k_{\min}\big)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[v(Z)^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}{\big(k_{\min}\leq X\leq k_{\min}+c^{*}a\sigma\big)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[v(Z)^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}{\big(k_{\min}+c^{*}a\sigma<X\leq n-2\big)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[v(Z)^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}{\big(X\geq n-1\big)}\right]

\displaystyle=:\text{I}+\text{II}+\text{III}.

(43)

We control each term separately.

Term III.
We use the inequality
\log(ab)\leq 2a\log(b)
when
a>1
and
b\geq 2
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\log(b)\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(b+1\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{ab}{a}+(1-\frac{1}{a})\cdot 1\right)\geq\frac{1}{2a}\log\left(ab\right)

by concavity of the logarithm.
Now, noting that
n(1-p)\geq p
, we have

III

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[v\!\left(\frac{X-np}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}{\big(X\geq n-1\big)}\right]

\displaystyle=v\!\left(\frac{n(1-p)-1}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}np^{n-1}(1-p)+v\!\left(\frac{n(1-p)}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}p^{n}

\displaystyle\leq 2v\!\left(\frac{n(1-p)}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}np^{n-1}(1-p)

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{\sigma}{p}\right)^{\alpha+2}\log^{\alpha}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)p^{n}

\displaystyle=2\left(\sqrt{\frac{n(1-p)}{p}}\right)^{\alpha+2}p^{n-\alpha}

\displaystyle=\leq 2\exp\left(\frac{\alpha+2}{2}\left(\log(n(1-p))+\log\Big(\frac{1}{p}\Big)\right)-(n-\alpha)\log\Big(\frac{1}{p}\Big)\right).

Now, recall that we have
\sqrt{n/4}\geq\sigma\geq a\geq C_{\alpha}
.
Therefore, we have
\frac{\alpha+2}{2}\log(n(1-p))\leq\frac{n-\alpha}{4}\log\left(1/p\right)
and
\frac{\alpha+2}{2}\log(1/p)\leq\frac{n-\alpha}{4}\log\left(1/p\right)
provided
C_{\alpha}
is sufficiently large. Therefore,

\displaystyle\text{III}\leq 2\exp\left(-n/2\right)\leq 2e^{-\frac{a^{2}}{2}}\leq 4a\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq a\right).

(44)

In the last line, we used Theorem 2.1 from
[
62
]
, which states that whenever
p\leq\frac{1}{4}
, it holds that
\mathbb{P}(Z\geq a)\geq 1-\Phi(a)\geq\frac{1}{2a}e^{-\frac{a^{2}}{2}}
for
a\geq 2
where
\Phi(a)
denotes the cumulative distribution function of the standard normal distribution.

Term I.
Note that, if
k\in[k_{\min},k_{\min}+c^{*}a\sigma]
, then

\displaystyle v\left(\frac{k-np}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq v\left(\frac{k_{\min}+c^{*}a\sigma-np}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=v\left(\frac{\lceil np+a\sigma\rceil+c^{*}a\sigma-np}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq v\left(\frac{(1+c^{*})a\sigma+1}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq v\Big((1+\frac{3}{2}c^{*})a\Big)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq(1+2c^{*})^{\alpha}\,v(a)^{\alpha}

provided the constant
C_{\alpha}>0
is chosen to be large enough.
Therefore, we obtain

I

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[v\!\left(\frac{X-np}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}{\big(k_{\min}\leq X\leq k_{\min}+c^{*}a\sigma\big)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1+2c^{*})^{\alpha}v(a)^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq a\right).

(45)

Term II.

We let
j_{0}=\lfloor c^{*}a\sigma\rfloor+1
and
k^{\prime}=k_{\min}+j_{0}
, so that the event
X>k_{\min}+c^{*}a\sigma
is equivalent to
X\geq k^{\prime}
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\text{II}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=k^{\prime}}^{n-2}v\left(\frac{k-np}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\frac{n!}{k!(n-k)!}p^{k}(1-p)^{n-k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=k^{\prime}}^{n-2}v\left(\frac{k-np}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}\left(\frac{n(1-p)}{n-k}\right)^{n-k}\left(\frac{np}{k}\right)^{k}\qquad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_Stirling}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{k=k^{\prime}}^{n-2}v\left(\frac{k-np}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}\left(1-\frac{k-np}{n(1-p)}\right)^{-n+k}\left(1+\frac{k-np}{np}\right)^{-k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n-k_{\min}-2}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j+1}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k_{\min}-j)(k_{\min}+j)}}

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{k_{\min}+j-np}{n(1-p)}\right)^{-n+k_{\min}+j}\left(1+\frac{k_{\min}+j-np}{np}\right)^{-k_{\min}-j}

\displaystyle=:\tilde{C}\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n-k_{\min}-2}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k_{\min}-j)(k_{\min}+j)}}\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j+1}{n(1-p)}\right)^{-n+k_{\min}+j}\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)^{-k_{\min}-j},

where
\tilde{C}=2\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}
and where we used the fact that
v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j+1}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\leq 2v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}

provided that
\sigma\geq a\geq C_{\alpha}
for some large enough constant
C_{\alpha}
.
Moreover, we note that

\displaystyle\quad\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j+1}{n(1-p)}\right)^{-n+k_{\min}+j}\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)^{-k_{\min}-j}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-(n-k_{\min}-j)\log\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j+1}{n(1-p)}\right)-(k_{\min}+j)\log\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-(n-np-a\sigma-j)\log\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j+1}{n(1-p)}\right)-(np+a\sigma+j)\log\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-n(1-p)\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j+1}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right).

We now argue that the latter quantity is at most a constant times the quantity below:

\displaystyle\exp\left(-n(1-p)\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right).

Indeed, the ratio of the two is

\displaystyle\exp\left(-n(1-p)\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{\frac{1}{n(1-p)}}{1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}}\right)\right),

and since
j\leq n-k_{\min}-2\leq n(1-p)-a\sigma-2
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{1}{n(1-p)}}{1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}}\leq\frac{\frac{1}{n(1-p)}}{\frac{2}{n(1-p)}}=\frac{1}{2}.

Using the inequality
\log(1-x)\geq-2x
for
x\in(0,1/2)
, the above ratio is controlled as

\displaystyle\exp\left(-n(1-p)\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{\frac{1}{n(1-p)}}{1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}}\right)\right)\leq\exp(2).

Combining these calculations, we obtain

II

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n-k_{\min}-2}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k_{\min}-j)(k_{\min}+j)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(-n(1-p)\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{C}\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n-k_{\min}-2}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k_{\min}-j)(k_{\min}+j)}}\exp\left(-n(1-p)h\left(-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\,h\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

where we have defined the Bennett function

\displaystyle h(x)=(1+x)\log\left(1+x\right)-x,\qquad\forall x>-1.

Now, we define the sets of indices
J_{1}=\{j_{0},\dots,\lfloor 2\sigma^{2}\rfloor\}
and
J_{2}=\{\lfloor 2\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1,\dots,n-k_{\min}-2\rfloor\}
.
We have

II

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\sum_{j\in J_{1}}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k_{\min}-j)(k_{\min}+j)}}\exp\left(-n(1-p)h\left(-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\,h\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\tilde{C}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k_{\min}-j)(k_{\min}+j)}}\exp\left(-n(1-p)h\left(-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\,h\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=:\text{I\hskip 0.56905ptI}_{J_{1}}+\text{I\hskip 0.56905ptI}_{J_{2}}

(46)

We now control these two terms separately.
Suppose first that
j\in J_{1}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\land\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\leq\frac{a\sigma+2\sigma^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq 3,

and, since
j\leq\sigma^{2}\leq n/4
, we also have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k_{\min}-j)(k_{\min}+j)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-np-a\sigma-1-n/4)(k_{\min}+j)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n/4-1)(k_{\min}+j)}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{\frac{1}{k_{\min}+j}}.

In the last line, we used the fact that, since
\sqrt{n/4}\geq\sigma\geq a\geq C_{\alpha}
, we have
n\geq 4C_{\alpha}^{2}
, so that
\frac{n}{n/4-1}\leq 9
for
C_{\alpha}
large enough.
Moreover, we have
h(x)\geq x^{2}/5
for any
x\in(-1,3]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3\tilde{C}}\text{I\hskip 0.56905ptI}_{J_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in J_{1}}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{1}{k_{\min}+j}}\exp\left(-n(1-p)h\left(-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\,h\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in J_{1}}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{1}{k_{\min}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{5}\frac{(a\sigma+j)^{2}}{n(1-p)}-\frac{1}{5}\frac{(a\sigma+j)^{2}}{np}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in J_{1}}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{1}{k_{\min}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{5}\frac{(a\sigma+j)^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right).

Note that the function
x\mapsto x^{\alpha}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{5}}
is decreasing over
[\sqrt{5\alpha/2},\infty)
. Therefore, choosing
C_{\alpha}
large enough again, and letting
\tilde{C}
denote a constant whose value may change in each appearance, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3\tilde{C}}\text{I\hskip 0.56905ptI}_{J_{1}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in J_{1}}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{1}{k_{\min}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{5}\frac{(a\sigma+j)^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{k_{\min}}}\int_{j_{0}}^{\infty}v\left(\frac{a\sigma+x}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}e^{-\frac{(a\sigma+x)^{2}}{5\sigma^{2}}}dx\qquad\text{ where }j_{0}-1=\lfloor c^{*}a\sigma\rfloor

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{k_{\min}}}\int_{\sqrt{\frac{2}{5}}\left(a+\frac{1}{\sigma}\lfloor c^{*}a\sigma\rfloor\right)}^{\infty}\left(\frac{5}{2}\right)^{1/2}v\left(\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}}y\right)^{\alpha}e^{-\frac{y^{2}}{2}}dy

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{k_{\min}}}\int_{a}^{\infty}v\left(y\right)^{\alpha}e^{-\frac{y^{2}}{2}}dy\qquad\text{ by taking $c^{*}$ large enough}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{k_{\min}}}a^{\alpha-1}\log(1+a)^{\alpha}\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\right)\qquad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian_tail_momentk_ellt}}

so that

\displaystyle\text{I\hskip 0.56905ptI}_{J_{1}}\leq C_{J_{1}}v(a)^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq a\right),

(47)

for some constant
C_{J_{1}}>0
, where we used the fact that
\sigma\leq\sqrt{k_{\min}}
and Theorem 2.1 from
[
62
]
.

Now, we turn to the term
\text{I\hskip 0.56905ptI}_{J_{2}}
.
Note that, when
j\in J_{2}
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\geq\frac{a\sigma+2np(1-p)}{np}\geq 2(1-p)\geq\frac{3}{2}.

Moreover, since
j\geq\sigma^{2}
, we also have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k_{\min}-j}}e^{-j/4}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{k_{\min}+j}{n}\right)^{-1/2}e^{-j/4}=\left(1-x\right)^{-1/2}e^{(-nx+k_{\min})/4}

where
x=(k_{\min}+j)/n\in[2p,1-\frac{2}{n}]
.
We now prove that the function
g:x\mapsto\left(1-x\right)^{-1/2}e^{-nx+k_{\min}}
is bounded over the interval
[2p,1-\frac{2}{n}]
independently of
n
.
Indeed, the derivative of
\log g
satisfies

\displaystyle(\log g)^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{2(1-x)}-n/4,

so that
g
is decreasing over
[2p,1-\frac{2}{n}]
.
It now suffices to evaluate
g(2p)

\displaystyle g(2p)

\displaystyle=\left(1-2p\right)^{-1/2}\exp\left(-2np+k_{\min}\right)\leq\left(1/2\right)^{-1/2}\exp\left(-2np+np+a\sigma+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1/2\right)^{-1/2}\exp\left(-\sigma^{2}/2\right)\leq\left(1/2\right)^{-1/2}\exp\left(-C_{\alpha}^{2}/2\right).

Therefore, there exists a constant
\bar{c}
such that, for any
n
and any
j\in J_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k_{\min}-j}}e^{-j}\leq\bar{c}.

By the inequality
h(x)\geq\frac{x}{2}

that holds for any
x\geq 3/2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{C}}\text{I\hskip 0.56905ptI}_{J_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in J_{2}}v\left(a+\frac{j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(k_{\min}+j)(n-k_{\min}-j)}}\exp\left(-n(1-p)h\left(-\frac{a\sigma+j}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\,h\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in J_{2}}v\left(a+\frac{j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{j}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k_{\min}-j}}\exp\left(-np\,h\left(\frac{a\sigma+j}{np}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in J_{2}}v\left(\frac{2j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{j}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k_{\min}-j}}\exp\left(-\frac{a\sigma+j}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\bar{c}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}v\left(\frac{j}{\sigma}\right)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{j}}\exp\left(-\frac{j}{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{C}}{\sigma^{\alpha}}\int_{\lfloor 2\sigma^{2}\rfloor}^{\infty}x^{\alpha-1/2}\log(1+x)^{\alpha}\exp\left(-x/4\right)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{C}}{\sigma^{\alpha}}\int_{\lfloor 2\sigma^{2}\rfloor/4}\left(4y\right)^{\alpha-1/2}\log(1+4y)^{\alpha}\exp\left(-y\right)4dy

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{C}}{\sigma^{\alpha}}\int_{\lfloor 2\sigma^{2}\rfloor/4}y^{\alpha-1/2}\log(1+y)^{\alpha}\exp\left(-y\right)4dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{C}}{\sigma^{\alpha}}\left(\frac{\lfloor 2\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{4}\right)^{\alpha-1/2}\log\left(1+\frac{\lfloor 2\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{4}\right)^{\alpha}\exp\left(-\frac{\lfloor 2\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{4}\right)\qquad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_moment_exponential}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{C}}{\sigma^{\alpha}}\left(\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{4}\right)^{\alpha-1/2}\log\left(1+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{4}\right)^{\alpha}\exp\left(-\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{J_{2}}\frac{v(\sigma)^{\alpha}}{\sigma}\exp\left(-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\right)\qquad\text{ for some constant }C_{J_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{J_{2}}\frac{v(a)^{\alpha}}{a}\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{J_{2}}v(a)^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq a\right)

(48)

where the last inequality follows from Theorem 2.1 from
[
62
]
.

To conclude the proof of the lemma, it remains to combine equations (
43
), (
44
), (
45
), (
46
), (
47
) and (
48
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{\alpha}|Z\geq a\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{\alpha}\,\mathbf{1}{\big(Z\geq a\big)}\right]}{\mathbb{P}(Z\geq a)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\text{I}+\text{II}+\text{III}}{\mathbb{P}(Z\geq A)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\text{I}+\text{II}_{J_{1}}+\text{II}_{J_{2}}+\text{III}}{\mathbb{P}(Z\geq A)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1+2c^{*})^{\alpha}v(a)^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq v(a)\right)+C_{J_{1}}v(a)^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq a\right)+C_{J_{2}}v(a)^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq a\right)+4v(a)\mathbb{P}(Z\geq a)}{\mathbb{P}(Z\geq a)}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}v(a)^{\alpha}

for some constant
\bar{C}
and
where we used
v(a)\leq a^{\alpha}
since
a,\alpha\geq 1
.
This completes the proof.

∎

We recall that the Bennett function is defined as

\displaystyle h_{B}(x)=(1+x)\log(1+x)-x,\qquad\forall x>-1.

(49)

\displaystyle h_{B}(-1)=1.

(50)

Now, we let
Y\sim\operatorname{Bin}(n,p_{0})
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
and define

\displaystyle w(x)

\displaystyle=n(1-p_{0})\,h_{B}\!\left(-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+np_{0}\,h_{B}\!\left(\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right),\forall x\in[0,n]

(51)

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=(Y-np_{0})/\sigma,\qquad\text{ where }\sigma=\sqrt{np_{0}(1-p_{0})}

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=w(Y)

\displaystyle\nu_{a}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[W|Z\geq a\right]

(52)

\displaystyle\gamma_{a}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[W^{2}|Z\geq a\right].

(53)

We deduce the following corollary.

Corollary 1
.

Recall the definitions of the functions
\nu
and
\gamma
from (
52
) and (
53
). There exist sufficiently large constants
C,\bar{C}
such that, for any
a\in[C,\sigma]
, it holds that
\nu_{a}\leq\bar{C}\sigma a\log\left(1+\frac{a}{\sigma}\right)\leq\bar{C}a^{2}
and
\gamma_{a}\leq\bar{C}\sigma a^{2}\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{a}{\sigma}\right)\leq\bar{C}a^{4}
.

Lemma 19
.

Let
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
and
p_{0}\in(0,1/4)
.
Let
Y_{1},\dots,Y_{m}\overset{iid}{\sim}\operatorname{Bin}(n,p_{0})
and for any
j\in\{1,\dots,m\}
define

Z_{j}=(Y_{j}-np_{0})/\sigma,\text{ where }\sigma=\sqrt{np_{0}(1-p_{0})}.

For any
j\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, define
W_{j}=w(Y_{j})
where
w
is defined in (
51
) and recall the definition of
\nu_{a}
from (
52
) for
a>0
.
Then there exist universal constants
C,C^{*},c,c^{\prime}>0
such that for any
a\in[C,c\sigma]
and
x>0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(W_{j}-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\left\{Z_{j}\geq a\right\}}\geq C^{*}\left(\sqrt{me^{-c^{\prime}a^{2}}x}+x\right)\right)\leq e^{-x}.

Proof of Lemma
19
.

In this proof, we will write
p
rather than
p_{0}
to alleviate the notation.
Let
Y\sim\operatorname{Bin}(n,p)
and
Z=(Y-np)/\sigma
, and consider
X=\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\{Z\geq a\}}
where

\displaystyle W=n(1-p)\,h_{B}\!\left(-\frac{Y-np}{n(1-p)}\right)+np\,h_{B}\!\left(\frac{Y-np}{np}\right).

We first derive a bound for the moment-generating function
\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\lambda X}\right)
. Since
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\lambda X}\right)=1+\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\lambda X}-1-\lambda X\right).

By the deterministic bound

e^{x}-1-x\leq\begin{cases}(-x)\wedge x^{2},&\text{ if }x<0\\
x^{2},&\text{ if }0\leq x\leq 1\\
e^{x},&\text{ if }x\geq 1\end{cases}

we have

\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\lambda X}-1-\lambda X\right)\leq\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(X^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{X<0\}}\right)+\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(X^{2}\mathbf{1}{\{0\leq X\leq 1/\lambda\}}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\lambda X}\mathbf{1}_{\{X\geq 1/\lambda\}}\right)

and we will bound the three terms separately. By Corollary
1
, the first term is bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\big(|X|\land X^{2}\big)\mathbf{1}_{\{X<0\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(X<0)\Big[\big|\nu_{a}-w(np+a\sigma)\big|\land\big(\nu_{a}-w(np+a\sigma)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(X<0)\Big[\big|\nu_{a}-\frac{a^{2}}{8}\big|\land\big(\nu_{a}-\frac{a^{2}}{8}\big)^{2}\Big]\qquad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_w_quadratic}.2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(Z\geq a)\left(\nu_{a}-\frac{a^{2}}{8}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\bar{C}-1/8)^{2}a^{4}\mathbb{P}\left(Y-np\geq a\sigma\right)\qquad\text{ by Corollary~\ref{cor_lemma17_LiuGaoSamworth}}

\displaystyle\leq(\bar{C}-1/8)^{2}a^{4}\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}/2}{1+\frac{a/\sigma}{3}}\right)\qquad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_rescalen_Bernstein}}

\displaystyle=(\bar{C}-1/8)^{2}a^{4}\exp\left(-\frac{3a^{2}}{8}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}}{4}\right)

provided the universal constant
C
such that
a\geq C
is chosen large enough depending on
\bar{C}
. We now bound the second term. Recalling the definition of the function
\gamma
from (
53
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(X^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{0<X\leq 1/\lambda\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(X^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{X>0\}}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{Z>\sqrt{\nu_{a}}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[W^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{Z>\sqrt{\nu_{a}}\}}\right]+2\nu_{a}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(Z>\sqrt{\nu_{a}}\right)

\displaystyle=2\left(\gamma_{\sqrt{\nu_{a}}}+\nu_{a}^{2}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(Z>\sqrt{\nu_{a}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\bar{C}\nu_{a}^{2}+\nu_{a}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\nu_{a}/2}{1+\frac{\sqrt{\nu_{a}}}{3\sigma}}\right)\qquad\text{ by Lemmas~\ref{lemma17_LiuGaoSamworth} and~\ref{lem_rescalen_Bernstein} }

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\bar{C}^{3}+\bar{C}^{2}\right)a^{4}\exp\left(-\frac{\nu_{a}/2}{1+\sqrt{\bar{C}}/3}\right)\quad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lemma17_LiuGaoSamworth} and }a\leq\sigma

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\nu_{a}}{2\bar{C}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}}{2\bar{C}}\right)

provided the universal constant
C
such that
a\geq C
is chosen large enough depending on
\bar{C}
.

Finally we control the third term.
We note that the event
X\geq\frac{1}{\lambda}
is equivalent to
W\geq\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda}
.
We let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
be the unique integer larger than or equal to
np
such that the event
\big\{W\geq\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda}
and
Z\geq 0\big\}
is equivalent to
\{Y\geq k_{0}\}
.
This integer exists by Lemma
26
since
w
is increasing over
[np,n]
by Lemma
26
,
w(np+a\sigma)=0
,
\lim_{x\to n}w(x)=2n\log(1/p)>\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda}
for
\lambda\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\nu_{a}\leq\bar{C}a^{2}\leq\bar{C}c^{2}\sigma^{2}\leq\bar{C}cn<2n\log(1/p)
for
c
chosen sufficiently small depending on
\bar{C}
.
Moreover, we also have
k_{0}\geq np+a\sigma
since

w(np+a\sigma)\leq a^{2}<\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda}
.
Now, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\lambda X}\mathbf{1}{\big(X\geq 1/\lambda\big)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda(W-\nu_{a})\right)\mathbf{1}{\big(W\geq\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\big)}\right]

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{n}e^{\lambda w(k)}\mathbb{P}(Y=k)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}e^{\lambda w(n)}\mathbb{P}(Y=n)+e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}e^{\lambda w(k_{0})}\mathbb{P}(Y=k_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{2\pi}c_{0}^{2}}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{n-1}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\left((2\lambda-1)\left\{n(1-p)h_{B}\left(-\frac{k-np}{n(1-p)}\right)+np\,h_{B}\left(\frac{k-np}{np}\right)\right\}\right)

(by Lemma
25
)

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}e^{\lambda w(n)}\mathbb{P}(Y=n)+e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}e^{\lambda w(k_{0})}\mathbb{P}(Y=k_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{2\pi}c_{0}^{2}}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\sum_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k)}

(54)

where we have defined
I_{1}=\{k_{0}+1,\dots,\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor\}
and
I_{2}=\{\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1,\dots,n-1\}
for some constant
C^{*}
that will be adjusted later.
We first compute the sum over the set of indices
I_{1}
.
Below, we use the notation
C^{\prime}
to denote a constant whose value may change in each appearance.
Using the change of variables

\displaystyle y=w(x)

\displaystyle dx=\frac{dy}{w^{\prime}\circ w^{-1}(y)}=\left(\log\left(\frac{w^{-1}(y)}{np}\frac{n(1-p)}{n-w^{-1}(y)}\right)\right)^{-1}dy

we have

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{I_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k)}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int_{k_{0}}^{\lfloor C^{*}\sigma\rfloor+1}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-x)x}}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})w(x)}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\sigma}\int_{k_{0}}^{\lfloor C^{*}\sigma\rfloor+1}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})w(x)}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{\prime}}{\sigma}\int_{w(k_{0})}^{w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1)}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})y}\left(\log\left(\frac{w^{-1}(y)}{np}\frac{n(1-p)}{n-w^{-1}(y)}\right)\right)^{-1}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{\prime}}{\sigma}\int_{w(k_{0})}^{w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1)}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})y}\left(\log\left(\frac{w^{-1}(y)}{np}\frac{1}{1-\frac{w^{-1}(y)-np}{n(1-p)}}\right)\right)^{-1}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\sigma}\int_{w(k_{0})}^{w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1)}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})y}\left(\log\left(\frac{w^{-1}(y)}{np}\left(1+\frac{w^{-1}(y)-np}{n(1-p)}\right)\right)\right)^{-1}dy.

Moreover, we have

w(x)\leq\frac{(x-np)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}
for any
x\in[np,n]
.
Therefore,
w^{-1}(y)\geq np+\sigma\sqrt{2y}
. Therefore, we can further control the display above as

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{I_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\sigma}\int_{w(k_{0})}^{w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1)}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})y}\left(\log\left(\left(1+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{2y}}{np}\right)\left(1+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{2y}}{n(1-p)}\right)\right)\right)^{-1}dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\sigma}\int_{w(k_{0})}^{w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1)}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})y}\left(\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{2y}}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{-1}dy.

Moreover, we have
w(x)\leq\frac{(x-np)^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}
for any
x>0
by Lemma
32
so that
w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1)\leq C^{\prime}\sigma^{2}
, which yields that, for some constant
c^{\prime}
that depends on
C^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{2y}}{\sigma}\right)\geq c^{\prime}\frac{\sqrt{y}}{\sigma},\qquad\forall y\in\left[{w(k_{0})},{w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1)}\right].

We obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\sigma}\int_{w(k_{0})}^{w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor+1)}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})y}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{y}}dy

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}-\lambda)w(k_{0})}^{\infty}e^{-z}\frac{1}{\sqrt{z}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1/2-\lambda}}dz

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int_{(\frac{1}{2}-\lambda)(\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda})}^{\infty}e^{-z}\frac{1}{\sqrt{z}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1/2-\lambda}}dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\exp\left(-(\frac{1}{2}-\lambda)(\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda})\right)}{\sqrt{(\frac{1}{2}-\lambda)(\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda})}},

which yields

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{2\pi}c_{0}^{2}}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\sum_{I_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k)}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{2\pi}c_{0}^{2}}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\frac{C^{\prime}\exp\left(-(\frac{1}{2}-\lambda)(\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda})\right)}{\sqrt{(\frac{1}{2}-\lambda)(\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda})}}

(55)

We now show that, for any
k\in I_{2}\setminus\{n-1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k-1)(k+1)}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k+1)}\leq\sqrt{2}e^{-2}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k)}.

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k-1)(k+1)}}\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}.

Moreover, since the function
w
is increasing and convex over
[np,n)
by Lemma
26
, we have

\displaystyle w(k+1)-w(k)\geq w^{\prime}(k)

\displaystyle\geq 2\log\left(\frac{k}{np}\frac{n(1-p)}{n-k}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 2\log\left(\frac{k_{0}}{np}\frac{n(1-p)}{n-k_{0}}\right)\qquad\text{ since $w^{\prime}$ is increasing over }[np,n]

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(\frac{np+\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{np}\frac{n(1-p)}{n-np-\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor}\right)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\left(1+\frac{\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{np}\right)\frac{1}{1-\frac{\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{n(1-p)}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(\left(1+\frac{\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{np}\right)\left(1+\frac{\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{n(1-p)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(1+\frac{\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor}{\sigma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 8

for
C^{*}
larger than an absolute constant.
For
\lambda\leq 1/4
, we therefore obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k-1)(k+1)}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k+1)}\leq\sqrt{2}e^{-2}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k)}.

Therefore, the sum over
I_{2}
can be controlled as by a geometric series, and we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{2\pi}c_{0}^{2}}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\sum_{k\in I_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(k)}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor)\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor}}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)w(\lfloor C^{*}\sigma^{2}\rfloor)}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\frac{1}{\sigma}e^{\left(\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\nu_{a}+1/\lambda\right)}

(56)

provided
C^{*}
is large enough.

To conclude, it remains to control the terms
e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}e^{\lambda w(n)}\mathbb{P}(Y=n)
and
e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}e^{\lambda w(k_{0})}\mathbb{P}(Y=k_{0})
.
For
k\in\{k_{0},n\}
, we have

\displaystyle\quad e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}e^{\lambda w(k)}\mathbb{P}(Y=k)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}e^{\lambda w(k)}\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}\exp\left(-n(1-p)\,h_{B}\!\left(-\frac{k-np}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\,\,h_{B}\!\left(\frac{k-np}{np}\right)\right)\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_Stirling}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{k}}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})w(k)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\lambda\nu_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}}}e^{(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})(\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda})}

(57)

by definition of
k_{0}
.
Combining equations (
54
), (
55
), (
56
) and (
57
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\lambda X}\mathbf{1}{\big(X\geq 1/\lambda\big)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\lambda\nu_{a}+(\lambda-\frac{1}{2})(\nu_{a}+\frac{1}{\lambda})}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\frac{\nu_{a}}{2}-\frac{1}{2\lambda}}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{2}e^{-\frac{\nu_{a}}{2}}.

Assembling these calculations, we have for
\lambda\in[0,M]

\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda X}\leq 1+C^{\prime}\lambda^{2}e^{-ca^{2}}\leq\exp\left(C^{\prime}\lambda^{2}e^{-ca^{2}}\right)

where
C^{\prime}>0
is a constant that depends on
\bar{C}
. Then, for any
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(W_{j}-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\left\{Z_{j}\geq a\right\}}>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\lambda\leq M}e^{-\lambda t}\left(\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda X}\right)^{m}\leq\inf_{\lambda\leq M}\exp\left(-\lambda t+C^{\prime}m\lambda^{2}e^{-ca^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{-\left(\frac{Mt^{2}e^{ca^{2}}}{C^{\prime}m}\wedge\frac{t}{2M}\right)\right\}

Setting
t=C^{*}\left(\sqrt{me^{-ca^{2}}x}+x\right)
for
C^{*}
sufficiently large, we obtain the desired result.
∎

Lemma 20
.

Let
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k\leq n/2
,
p_{0}\in(0,1/4)
and
p_{1}\in[0,1]
.
Let
Y=A+B
where
A\sim\operatorname{Bin}(k,p_{1})
and
B\sim\operatorname{Bin}(n-k,p_{0})
are independent and define

Z=(Y-np_{0})/\sigma,\text{ where }\sigma=\sqrt{np_{0}(1-p_{0})}.

Let
w
denote the function defined in (
51
) and let
W=w(Y)
.
Let
\theta=\frac{k(p_{1}-p_{0})}{\sigma}
and recall the definition of
\nu_{a}
from (
52
) for
a>0
.
Then there exist universal constants
\tilde{c},c,C>0
such that, if
kp_{1}\geq C
, then for any
a\in[C,c\sigma]
,

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right\}\begin{cases}=0,&\text{ if }\theta=0\\[5.0pt]
\geq\tilde{c}\,\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right),&\text{ if }\theta\geq Ca.\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
20
.

The fact that
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right\}=0
when
\theta=0
follows by definition of
\nu_{a}
.

Suppose now that
\theta\geq Ca
. Recalling that the function
w
is increasing over
[np_{0},n]
by Lemma
26
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[(W-\nu_{a})\mathbf{1}{\big(Z\geq a\big)}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\left[W\mathbf{1}{\big(Z\geq\theta\big)}\right]-\nu_{a}

\displaystyle=E\left[w(Y)\mathbf{1}{\big(Y\geq kp_{1}+(n-k)p_{0}\big)}\right]-\nu_{a}

\displaystyle\geq w(kp_{1}+(n-k)p_{0})\mathbb{P}\left(Y\geq kp_{1}+(n-k)p_{0}\right)-\bar{C}a^{2}\qquad\text{ by Corollary~\ref{cor_lemma17_LiuGaoSamworth}}

\displaystyle=c^{\prime}\mathbb{P}\left(Y\geq kp_{1}+(n-k)p_{0}\right)\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)-\bar{C}a^{2}\qquad\text{ for some $c^{\prime}>0$ by Corollary~\ref{cor_w_growth_w}.}

Moreover, the median
m_{n,p}
of a binomial distribution with parameters
n
and
p
satisfies
|m_{n,p}-np|<\log(2)
(see
[
34
]
, Theorem 2).
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Y\geq np_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(A\geq kp_{1}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(B\geq(n-k)p_{0}\right)

We show that
P\left(A\geq kp_{1}\right)\geq\frac{1}{4}
provided
kp_{1}\geq C
for some large enough constant
C>0
.
We have

\displaystyle P\left(A\geq kp_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(A\geq m_{k,p_{1}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(A=\lfloor kp_{1}\rfloor-1\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-\mathbb{P}\left(A=\lfloor kp_{1}\rfloor-1\right).

Moreover, letting
j=\lfloor kp_{1}\rfloor-1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(A=j\right)

\displaystyle={k\choose j}p_{1}^{j}(1-p_{1})^{k-j}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{k}{(k-j)j}}\left(\frac{k(1-p_{1})}{k-j}\right)^{k-j}\left(\frac{kp_{1}}{j}\right)^{j}\qquad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_Stirling}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\frac{2}{\sqrt{kp_{1}}}\left(1+\frac{kp_{1}-j}{j}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\frac{2}{\sqrt{kp_{1}}}\exp\left(kp_{1}-j\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}e^{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{kp_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}

provided
kp_{1}\geq C
, as claimed.
Similarly, we can show that
P\left(B\geq(n-k)p_{0}\right)\geq\frac{1}{4}
provided
(n-k)p_{0}\geq C
for some large enough constant
C>0
.
Therefore, we have proved
\mathbb{P}(Y\geq np_{1})\geq\frac{1}{16}
, which yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(W-\nu_{a})\mathbf{1}{\big(Z\geq a\big)}\right]\geq\frac{c^{\prime}}{16}\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)-\bar{C}a^{2}.

Moreover, since
\theta\geq Ca
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma Ca\log\left(1+\frac{Ca}{\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma Ca\log(2)\cdot\frac{Ca}{\sigma}\qquad\text{ provided $c\leq 1/C$}

\displaystyle\geq C^{2}\log(2)a^{2}.

We may now choose
C
large enough that
\bar{C}a^{2}\leq\frac{c^{\prime}}{32}\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)
,
which yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(W-\nu_{a})\mathbf{1}{\big(Z\geq a\big)}\right]\geq\frac{c^{\prime}}{32}\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)

and concludes the proof.

∎

Lemma 21
.

Let
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k\leq n/2
,
p_{0}\in(0,1/4)
and
p_{1}\in[0,1]
.
Let
Y=A+B
where
A\sim\operatorname{Bin}(k,p_{1})
and
B\sim\operatorname{Bin}(n-k,p_{0})
are independent and define

Z=(Y-np_{0})/\sigma,\text{ where }\sigma=\sqrt{np_{0}(1-p_{0})}.

Let
w
denote the function defined in (
51
) and let
W=w(Y)
.
Let
\theta=\frac{k(p_{1}-p_{0})}{\sigma}
and recall the definition of
\nu_{a}
from (
52
) for
a>0
.
Then there exist universal constants
C_{1},c,C>0
such that, if
kp_{1}\geq C
, then for any
a\in[C,c\sigma]
,

\operatorname{Var}\left\{\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right\}\leq\begin{cases}C_{1}\left(\sigma a\log\left(1+\frac{a}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}a^{2}\right)&\text{ if }\theta=0\\[5.0pt]
C_{1}(\sigma^{2}+\sigma\theta)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)&\text{ if }\theta\geq 2a.\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
21
.

Let
a\geq C
. In this proof, we use the notation
\tilde{C}
to denote a constant whose value may change in each appearance.
When
\theta=0
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\left\{\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[W^{2}\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right]-2\nu_{a}\mathbb{E}\left[W\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right]+\nu_{a}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq a\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma_{a}-\nu_{a}^{2}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq a\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}\left(\sigma a\log\left(1+\frac{a}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}a^{2}}{1+\frac{a/\sigma}{3}}\right)\qquad\text{ by Corollary~\ref{cor_lemma17_LiuGaoSamworth} and Lemma~\ref{lem_rescalen_Bernstein}}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}\left(\sigma a\log\left(1+\frac{a}{\sigma}\right)\right)^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}a^{2}\right).

For
\theta\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\operatorname{Var}\left\{\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{Var}\left\{\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\mid\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\operatorname{Var}\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(W-\nu_{a}\right)\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\mid\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(Z\geq a)\operatorname{Var}\left(W|Z\geq a\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}(Z<a)\mathbb{P}(Z\geq a)\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(W-\nu_{a}|Z\geq a\right)\right\}^{2}.

(58)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Z\geq a)\operatorname{Var}\left(W|Z\geq a\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\operatorname{Var}\left(W\mid\mathbf{1}{\{Z\geq a\}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{Var}\left(W\right)=\mathbb{E}[W^{2}]-\mathbb{E}^{2}[W].

(59)

Now, we recall that
\mathbb{E}Y=kp_{1}+(n-k)p_{0}
.
We observe that, for any
x\in[0,n]

\displaystyle w(x)

\displaystyle=(n-x)\log\left(\frac{n-x}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+x\log\left(\frac{x}{np_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=(n-x)\log\left(\frac{n-\mathbb{E}Y}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+x\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{np_{0}}\right)+(n-x)\log\left(\frac{n-x}{n-\mathbb{E}Y}\right)+x\log\left(\frac{x}{\mathbb{E}Y}\right)

\displaystyle=:nD_{KL}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{n}\,\Big|\Big|\,p_{0}\right)+(x-\mathbb{E}Y)\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{np_{0}}\frac{n(1-p_{0})}{n-\mathbb{E}Y}\right)+w_{p_{1}}(x),

where
D_{KL}(p||q)=(1-p)\log\left(\frac{1-p}{1-q}\right)+p\log\left(\frac{p}{q}\right)
for any
p,q\in(0,1)
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(W)=nD_{KL}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{n}\,\Big|\Big|\,p_{0}\right)+\mathbb{E}(w_{p_{1}}(Y))

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{2}(W)\geq\left(nD_{KL}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{n}\,\Big|\Big|\,p_{0}\right)\right)^{2}+2nD_{KL}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{n}\,\Big|\Big|\,p_{0}\right)\mathbb{E}(w_{p_{1}}(Y)).

Moreover, using the inequality
ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
that holds true for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W^{2}]

\displaystyle=\left(nD_{KL}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{n}\,\Big|\Big|\,p_{0}\right)\right)^{2}+\mathbb{V}(Y)\log^{2}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{np_{0}}\frac{n(1-p_{0})}{n-\mathbb{E}Y}\right)+\mathbb{E}(w_{p_{1}}(Y)^{2})+2nD_{KL}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{n}\,\Big|\Big|\,p_{0}\right)\mathbb{E}(w_{p_{1}}(Y))

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)\log\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{np_{0}}\frac{n(1-p_{0})}{n-\mathbb{E}Y}\right)w_{p_{1}}(Y)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(nD_{KL}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{n}\,\Big|\Big|\,p_{0}\right)\right)^{2}+2\mathbb{V}(Y)\log^{2}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{np_{0}}\frac{n(1-p_{0})}{n-\mathbb{E}Y}\right)+2\mathbb{E}(w_{p_{1}}(Y)^{2})+2nD_{KL}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{n}\,\Big|\Big|\,p_{0}\right)\mathbb{E}(w_{p_{1}}(Y)).

Recalling
k\leq n/2
, we have
\frac{k(p_{1}-p_{0})}{n}\leq 1/2
. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(W)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{V}(Y)\log^{2}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{np_{0}}\frac{n(1-p_{0})}{n-\mathbb{E}Y}\right)+2\mathbb{E}(w_{p_{1}}(Y)^{2})

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{V}(Y)\log^{2}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}Y}{np_{0}}\frac{n(1-p_{0})}{n-\mathbb{E}Y}\right)+2\mathbb{E}\left(4\left(\frac{Y-\mathbb{E}Y}{\sigma(Y)}\right)^{4}\right)\qquad\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem_w_quadratic}.1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(kp_{1}+(n-k)p_{0}\right)\log^{2}\left(\left(1+\frac{k(p_{1}-p_{0})}{np_{0}}\right)\frac{n(1-p_{0})}{n(1-p_{0})-k(p_{1}-p_{0}))}\right)+C

\displaystyle\leq Cnp_{0}\left(1+\frac{k(p_{1}-p_{0})}{np_{0}}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{k(p_{1}-p_{0})}{np_{0}(1-p_{0})}\right)+C\qquad\text{since }\frac{k(p_{1}-p_{0})}{n(1-p_{0})}\leq 1/2

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sigma^{2}+\theta\sigma\right)\log^{2}\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right).

Finally, writing
Z=\theta+X
where
X=\frac{Y-kp_{1}-(n-k)p_{0}}{\sigma}
, we have by Lemma
18

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left(W-\nu_{a}|Z\geq a\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[w(kp_{1}+(n-k)p_{0}+\sigma X)\,\big|\,\theta+X\geq a\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[w(kp_{1}+(n-k)p_{0}+\sigma X)\,\big|\,X\geq a\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[(k(p_{1}-p_{0})+\sigma X)\log\left(1+\frac{k(p_{1}-p_{0})+\sigma X}{\sigma^{2}}\right)\,\big|\,X\geq a\Big]\quad\text{ by Corollary~\ref{cor_w_growth_w}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\left(\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma X\log\left(1+\frac{X}{\sigma}\right)\,\big|\,X\geq a\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\left(\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)+\sigma a\log\left(1+\frac{a}{\sigma}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right).

(60)

If
|\theta|\geq 2a
, notice that, by Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Z<a)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(X<-(\theta-a)\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}(a-\theta)^{2}}{1+\frac{(\theta-a)/\sigma}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}\big(1-{\bar{C}}^{-1}\big)^{2}\theta^{2}}{1+\frac{(1-{\bar{C}}^{-1})\theta/\sigma}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}/\theta^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{C}}{\sigma\theta\log\left(1+\frac{\theta}{\sigma}\right)}

(61)

if
\theta\geq C
for some large enough
C>0
.
The result follows by combining (
58
), (
59
), (
60
) and (
61
).

∎

Lemma 22
.

Let
Y\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and let
\alpha,\beta\geq 1
and
x>0
such that
x^{2}\geq 2(\alpha-1)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[Y^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{Y\geq x}\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2x^{\alpha-1}e^{-x^{2}/2}

provided
x^{2}\geq 2(\alpha-1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[Y^{\alpha}\log(1+Y)^{\beta}\mathbf{1}_{Y\geq x}\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2x^{\alpha-1}\log(1+x)^{\beta}e^{-x^{2}/2}

provided
x^{2}\geq 2(\alpha+\beta-1)
.

Proof of Lemma
22
.

By integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy

\displaystyle=x^{\alpha-1}e^{-x^{2}/2}+(\alpha\!-\!1)\!\int_{x}^{\infty}\!y^{\alpha-2}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy

\displaystyle\leq x^{\alpha-1}e^{-x^{2}/2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy~~\text{using that $y^{2}\geq 2(\alpha-1)$ over $[x,+\infty)$},

\displaystyle\text{so that }\quad\mathbb{E}\big[Y^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{Y\geq x}\big]

\displaystyle=\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy\leq 2x^{\alpha-1}e^{-x^{2}/2}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}\log(1+y)^{\beta}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy

\displaystyle=x^{\alpha-1}\log(1+y)^{\beta}e^{-x^{2}/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\!\int_{x}^{\infty}\!\left((\alpha\!-\!1)y^{\alpha-2}\log(1+y)^{\beta}+\beta\frac{y^{\alpha-1}}{1+y}\log(1+y)^{\beta-1}\right)e^{-y^{2}/2}dy

\displaystyle\leq x^{\alpha-1}\log(1+y)^{\beta}e^{-x^{2}/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\!\int_{x}^{\infty}\!(\alpha+\beta-1)y^{\alpha-2}\log(1+y)^{\beta}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy

\displaystyle\leq x^{\alpha-1}\log(1+y)^{\beta}e^{-x^{2}/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}\log(1+y)^{\beta}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy~~\text{using that $y^{2}\geq 2(\alpha+\beta-1)$ over $[x,+\infty)$},

\displaystyle\text{so that }\quad\mathbb{E}\big[v(Y)^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{Y\geq x}\big]

\displaystyle=\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}\log(1+y)^{\beta}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy\leq 2x^{\alpha-1}\log(1+y)^{\beta}e^{-x^{2}/2}.

∎

Lemma 23
.

For any
\alpha>0
and
x\geq 2\alpha
, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}e^{-y}dy\leq 2x^{\alpha}e^{-x}.

Proof of Lemma
23
.

By integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}e^{-y}dy\leq 2x^{\alpha}e^{-x}

\displaystyle=x^{\alpha}e^{-x}+\int_{x}^{\infty}\alpha x^{\alpha-1}e^{-x}dx

\displaystyle\leq x^{\alpha}e^{-x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{x}^{\infty}x^{\alpha}e^{-x}dx\qquad\text{ since }y\geq 2\alpha\text{ over }[x,\infty),

from which it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{\alpha}e^{-y}dy\leq 2x^{\alpha}e^{-x},

as claimed.
∎

Lemma 24
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
p\in(0,1/2)
.
Let
X\sim\operatorname{Bin}(n,p)
and
\sigma=\sqrt{np(1-p)}
.
For any
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{X-np}{\sigma}\geq t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{1+\frac{t}{3\sigma}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{X-np}{\sigma}\leq-t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{1+\frac{t}{3\sigma}}\right)

Proof.

The result follows by a direct application of the Bernstein inequality for binomial distributions.
∎

Lemma 25
.

There exist two absolute constants
C_{0},c_{0}
such that, for any
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k<n
and
p\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle{n\choose k}p^{k}(1-p)^{n-k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}\left(\frac{n(1-p)}{n-k}\right)^{n-k}\left(\frac{np}{k}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{0}}{c_{0}^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{(n-k)k}}\exp\left(-n(1-p)h_{B}\left(-\frac{k-np}{n(1-p)}\right)-np\,h_{B}\left(\frac{k-np}{np}\right)\right).

Proof.

We recall that, by the Stirling formula, we have

\displaystyle m!\sim\sqrt{2\pi m}\left(\frac{m}{e}\right)^{m},\qquad\text{ as }m\to\infty.

Since the function
m\in\mathbb{N}\mapsto\frac{m!}{\sqrt{2\pi m}\left(\frac{m}{e}\right)^{m}}
is positive and converges to
1
, there exist two absolute constants
c_{0},C_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle c_{0}\sqrt{2\pi m}\left(\frac{m}{e}\right)^{m}\leq m!\leq C_{0}\sqrt{2\pi m}\left(\frac{m}{e}\right)^{m},\qquad\forall m\in\mathbb{N}.

(62)

The result follows.
∎

Lemma 26
.

The function
w
defined in (
51
) is convex and increasing over
[np_{0},n)
.

Proof.

The derivative of
w
is given by

\displaystyle w^{\prime}(x)=2\log\left(\frac{x}{np_{0}}\frac{n(1-p_{0})}{n-x}\right),\quad\forall x\in[np_{0},n]

which is positive if
x>np_{0}
.
The second derivative of
w
is given by

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n-x}+\frac{2}{x}

which is positive for any
x\in[np_{0},n)
.
∎

C.2
Proofs of propositions in the main text

We begin by restating and proving Proposition 1.

Proposition 4
.

Let
c>0
. There exists a constant
C>0
such that if

\frac{C}{k_{1}k_{2}}\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)\leq p_{0}\leq\frac{1}{4},

then it holds

\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(\text{$\mathbf{G}$ has an empty $k_{1}\times k_{2}$ bipartite subgraph}\right)\leq c.

Here, we say that a subgraph
\mathbf{G}_{1}
of
\mathbf{G}
is empty if all the vertices in
\mathbf{G}_{1}
has degree equal to 0.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(\text{$\mathbf{G}$ has an empty $k_{1}\times k_{2}$ bipartite subgraph}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(\exists(K_{1},K_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{k_{1}}(n_{1})\times\mathcal{P}_{k_{2}}(n_{2}),\forall(i,j)\in K_{1}\times K_{2}:A_{ij}=0\right)

\displaystyle\leq{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\mathbb{P}\left(\text{Bin}(k_{1}k_{2},p_{0})=0\right)

\displaystyle={n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}(1-p_{0})^{k_{1}k_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}e^{-k_{1}k_{2}p_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\exp\left(-C\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-(C-1)\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)\right)\quad\text{(for $C>1$)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-(C-1)\right)

\displaystyle\leq c,

where the final inequality holds for
C
taken sufficiently large.
∎

Now we state and prove Proposition 2.

Proposition 5
.

Let
c>0
. There exists a constant
C>0
such that if

\frac{C}{n_{2}k_{1}}\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\leq p_{0}\leq\frac{1}{4},

then it holds

\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\exists I\in\mathcal{P}_{k_{1}}(n_{1}):\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}A_{ij}=0\Big)\leq c.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\Big(\exists I\in\mathcal{P}_{k_{1}}(n_{1}):\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}A_{ij}=0\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\mathbb{P}\left(\text{Bin}(k_{1}n_{2},p_{0})=0\right)

\displaystyle={n_{1}\choose k_{1}}(1-p_{0})^{k_{1}n_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}e^{-k_{1}n_{2}p_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\exp\left(-C\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-(C-1)\log\left(e{n_{1}\choose k_{1}}\right)\right)\quad\text{(for $C>1$)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-(C-1)\right)

\displaystyle\leq c,

where the final inequality holds for
C
taken sufficiently large.
∎

Finally, we state and prove Proposition 3.

Proposition 6
.

Suppose that
k_{1}^{2}\geq\bar{c}n_{1}k_{2}
for a constant
\bar{c}>0
and
k_{j}\leq c_{j}n_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
where
c_{1},c_{2}>0
are sufficiently small constants. Additionally, suppose that there exists a constant
\alpha>0
such that
n_{2}\geq k_{2}^{2+\alpha}
and that
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq e\log(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})
. Then it holds

\frac{(\delta^{*})^{2}}{p_{0}(1-p_{0})}\asymp\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log(n_{2})\right).

(63)

In particular, this reveals a phase transition at
\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log(n_{2})\asymp 1
.

Proof.

Notice that the assumption
\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\geq e\log(\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}})
can be made without loss of generality by Lemma
27
. Theorems 1 and 2 together imply

\frac{(\delta^{*})^{2}}{p_{0}(1-p_{0})}\asymp R.

Therefore, we need to show

R\asymp\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log(n_{2})\right).

Note that the assumption
n_{2}\geq k_{2}^{2+\alpha}
implies that
\phi_{21}=\infty
if
c_{2}
is large enough.
Then by Lemma
8
, it holds

R\asymp\psi_{21}\land\phi_{12}.

Furthermore,
n_{2}\geq k_{2}^{2+\alpha}
implies that
\log(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}})\asymp\log(n_{2})
. Using the inequality
\log(1+x)\leq x
which holds for any
x\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(e{n_{2}\choose k_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log(n_{2}/k_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{k_{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log(n_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{k_{2}}\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log(n_{2})

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{n_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{n_{2}}{k_{2}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\phi_{12}.

This implies
R\asymp\psi_{21}\land\phi_{12}=\psi_{21}\asymp\log\left(1+\frac{n_{1}k_{2}}{k_{1}^{2}}\log(n_{2})\right)
. The proof is complete.
∎

C.3
Miscellaneous analytic lemmas

Lemma 27
.

For any two real numbers
x,y>1
, at least one of the two inequalities
x\geq e\log(y)
or
y\geq e\log(x)
holds.

Proof of Lemma
27
.

We first prove a preliminary result: For any
x>1
, it holds that
x\geq e\log(e\log(x))
.
To see this, define the function
f:(1,\infty)\to\mathbb{R};~x\mapsto x-e\log(e\log(x))
. We have for any
x>1

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(x)=1-\frac{e}{x\log(x)}.

The only value
x^{*}
for which
f(x^{*})=0
is
x^{*}=e
, which implies that
f
is decreasing over
(1,e)
and increasing over
(e,\infty)
. Hence,
f
is minimized at
e
and its corresponding minimum value is

\displaystyle f(e)=e-e\log(e\log(e))=0.

This fact being established, assume now for the sake of contradiction that there exist two real numbers
x,y
such that
x<e\log(y)
and
y<e\log(x)
.
Then we obtain
x<e\log(e\log(x))
which is a contradiction. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 28
.

Let
X\sim\text{Bin}(n,p)
with
p\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Then for any
k\in\{1,...,n\}
, we have

\Pr(X=k)\leq\Big(\frac{2enp}{k}\Big)^{k}\exp\big(-np\big)

Proof.

Using the bound
{n\choose k}\leq\big(\frac{ne}{k}\big)^{k}
(Appendix A in
[
59
]
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr(X=k)

\displaystyle={n\choose k}p^{k}\big(1-p\big)^{n-k}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{npe}{k}\Big)^{k}\big(1-p\big)^{n-k}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{npe}{k}\Big)^{k}\big(1-p\big)^{n}\big(1-p\big)^{-k}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{npe}{k}\Big)^{k}\big(1-p\big)^{n}\big(1/2\big)^{-k}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{2enp}{k}\Big)^{k}\Big(1-p\Big)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{2enp}{k}\Big)^{k}\exp\big(-np\big)

where the final inequality uses
(1-x)^{b}\leq\exp(-xb)
for any
x,b\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma 29
.

Let
f:S\rightarrow(0,\infty)
be differentiable, where
S\subseteq(0,\infty)
is an interval. For
x\in S
, define
g(x)=\frac{1}{x}\log\big(f(x)\big)
and
g^{(1)}(x)=\frac{1}{x}\log\big(1+f(x)\big)
. If
g
is decreasing on
S
, then
g^{(1)}
is decreasing on
S
as well.

Proof.

Let
f^{\prime}(x)=\frac{\textrm{d}f}{\textrm{d}x}(x)
for any
x\in S
. By direct calculation, we have

\frac{\textrm{d}g}{\textrm{d}x}(x)=\frac{f^{\prime}(x)}{xf(x)}-\frac{\log\big(f(x)\big)}{x^{2}}.

If
g
is decreasing on
S
, then for each
x\in S
it holds

\frac{f^{\prime}(x)}{xf(x)}<\frac{\log\big(f(x)\big)}{x^{2}}.

Now calculating the derivative of
g^{(1)}
at any
x\in S
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\textrm{d}g^{(1)}}{\textrm{d}x}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{f^{\prime}(x)}{x\big(1+f(x)\big)}-\frac{\log\big(1+f(x)\big)}{x^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{f^{\prime}(x)}{x\big(1+f(x)\big)}\frac{f(x)}{f(x)}-\frac{\log\big(1+f(x)\big)}{x^{2}}

\displaystyle<\frac{\log\big(f(x)\big)}{x^{2}}\frac{f(x)}{\big(1+f(x)\big)}-\frac{\log\big(1+f(x)\big)}{x^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 0

where the final inequality uses
f(x)\leq 1+f(x)
and
\log\big(f(x)\big)\leq\log\big(1+f(x)\big)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 30
.

Let
x,y>e
.

(i)

If
x/\log(x)\leq y/2
, then we have
x\leq y\log(y)
.

(ii)

If
x/\log(x)\geq y
, then we have
x\geq y\log(y)
.

Proof.

(i)

We prove the statement by contrapositive. Suppose that
x>y\log(y)
. Then since
t\mapsto\frac{t}{\log(t)}
is increasing over
(e,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{x}{\log(x)}\geq\frac{y\log(y)}{\log(y\log(y))}=\frac{y}{1+\frac{\log\log(y)}{\log(y)}}>\frac{y}{2}.

(ii)

Again, we prove the statement by contrapositive.
Suppose that
x/\log(x)<y.
Then since
t\mapsto\frac{t}{\log(t)}
is increasing over
(e,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{x}{\log(x)}<\frac{y\log(y)}{\log(y\log(y))}=\frac{y}{1+\frac{\log\log(y)}{\log(y)}}<y.

This completes the proof.

∎

Lemma 31
.

The following two properties hold.

(i)

For any
x\geq 0
and
c\in[0,1]
, we have
\log(1+cx)\geq c\log(1+x)
.

(ii)

For any
x\geq 0
and
C\geq 1
, we have
C\log(1+x)\geq\log(1+Cx)
.

Proof of Lemma
31
.

(i)

By concavity of the logarithm, we have
\log(1+cx)=\log\left((1-c)\cdot 1+c(1+x)\right)\geq(1-c)\log(1)+c\log(1+x)=c\log(1+x)
for any
c\in[0,1]
.

(ii)

We have seen above that
\log(1+cy)\geq c\log(1+y)
for any
y\geq 0
and
c\in[0,1]
.
Applying this with
y=Cx
and
c=1/C
yields the result.

∎

Lemma 32
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
p_{0}\in(0,1)
and
w
denote the function defined in (
51
). For any
x\in[0,n]
, the following properties hold.

1.

We have
w(x)\leq\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}
where
\sigma^{2}=np_{0}(1-p_{0})

2.

There exists a universal constant
c_{0}>0
such that, for any
x\in[np_{0},np_{0}+c_{0}\sigma^{2}]
, we have
w(x)\geq\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}
.

3.

For any
x\in[c_{0}\sigma^{2},n]
, we have
w(x)\asymp\sigma\frac{x-np_{0}}{\sigma}\log\left(1+\frac{x-np_{0}}{\sigma^{2}}\right).

Proof of Lemma
32
.

1.

Using the inequality
\log(1+x)\leq x
that holds for any
x\in[-1,\infty)
with the convention
\log(0)=-\infty
, we have, for any
x\in[0,n]

\displaystyle w(x)

\displaystyle=n(1-p_{0})\,h_{B}\!\left(-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+np_{0}\,h_{B}\!\left(\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=n(1-p_{0})\,\left\{\left(1-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+np_{0}\,\left\{\left(1+\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)-\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq n(1-p_{0})\,\left\{\left(1-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)\left(-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+np_{0}\,\left\{\left(1+\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)-\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{n(1-p_{0})}+\frac{2(x-np_{0})^{2}}{np_{0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}.

2.

For any
x\in[np_{0},np_{0}+c\sigma^{2}]
, we have
\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\lor\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\in[0,c]
. Choosing
c
small enough that
\log(1+t)\geq t-\frac{3t^{2}}{4}
for any
t\in[-c,c]
, we obtain that,

\displaystyle w(x)

\displaystyle=n(1-p_{0})\,h_{B}\!\left(-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+np_{0}\,h_{B}\!\left(\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=n(1-p_{0})\,\left\{\left(1-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+np_{0}\,\left\{\left(1+\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)-\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq n(1-p_{0})\,\left\{\left(1-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)\left(-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}-\frac{3}{4}\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{(n(1-p_{0}))^{2}}\right)+\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p)}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+np_{0}\,\left\{\left(1+\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}-\frac{3}{4}\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{(n(1-p_{0}))^{2}}\right)-\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq n(1-p_{0})\frac{1}{4}\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{(n(1-p_{0}))^{2}}+np_{0}\left[\frac{1}{4}\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{(np_{0})^{2}}-\frac{3}{4}\frac{(x-np_{0})^{3}}{(np_{0})^{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{8}\frac{(x-np_{0})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}

provided
c
is small enough.

3.

Assume now that
x\in[np_{0}+c_{0}\sigma^{2},n]
.
We have

\displaystyle n(1-p_{0})h_{B}\left(-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)

\displaystyle=n(1-p_{0})\,\left\{\left(1-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right)+\frac{x-np_{0}}{n(1-p_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle\in\big[0,x-np_{0}\big].

Moreover, using the relation
h_{B}(y)\asymp y\log(1+y)
that holds for any
y>0
, we have

\displaystyle np_{0}\,h_{B}\!\left(\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)\asymp(x-np_{0})\log\left(\frac{x}{np_{0}}\right)\geq\log\left(1+\frac{3c_{0}}{4}\right)(x-np_{0}).

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle w(x)

\displaystyle\asymp np_{0}\,h_{B}\!\left(\frac{x-np_{0}}{np_{0}}\right)\asymp(x-np_{0})\log\left(\frac{x}{np_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\sigma\frac{x-np_{0}}{\sigma}\log\left(1+\frac{x-np_{0}}{\sigma^{2}}\right).

∎

The following corollary follows by combining the properties from Lemma
32
.

Corollary 2
.

For any
x\in[np_{0},n]
, it holds that
w(x)\asymp\sigma z\log(1+z/\sigma)
where
z=\frac{x-np_{0}}{\sigma}
and
\sigma^{2}=np_{0}(1-p_{0})
.
